@pytest.mark.unit
def test_a_settlement_failure_is_noted_and_the_run_goes_on(tmp_path, monkeypatch, offline):
    """The analysts have spent their tokens by the time the join waits on settlement."""
    from tradingagents.reporting import write_report_tree

    graph = _graph(tmp_path, monkeypatch, ScriptedModel(structured=True))

    def locked():
        raise OSError("memory log is locked")

    monkeypatch.setattr(graph, "settle_all_pending", locked)
    final_state, rating = graph.propagate("NVDA", TRADE_DATE)

    assert rating == "Overweight"
    assert "could not be settled" in final_state["memory_note"]
    report = write_report_tree(final_state, "NVDA", tmp_path / "report").read_text()
    assert "Memory log: Past decisions could not be settled" in report

@pytest.mark.unit
def test_a_decision_without_claim_metadata_is_not_accepted():
    audit = audit_material_claims(_state(material_claims=None))

    assert audit["status"] == "NOT_ASSESSED"
    assert audit["status"] != "ACCEPTED"
    assert audit["reasons"] == ["NO_CLAIM_METADATA"]


@pytest.mark.unit
def test_unsupported_claims_stay_visible_and_labelled_unsupported(tmp_path):
    claims = _claims()
    claims[0]["source_id"] = "podcast_transcript"
    report = write_report_tree(_state(material_claims=claims), "NVDA", tmp_path).read_text(
        encoding="utf-8")

    assert "Close was 181.2." in report
    assert "UNSUPPORTED" in report and "UNKNOWN_SOURCE" in report

```

def test_the_report_names_each_claim_source_next_to_the_claim(tmp_path):

import os

_TRADINGAGENTS_HOME = os.path.join(os.path.expanduser("~"), ".tradingagents")

# Single source of truth for env-var → config-key overrides. To expose
# a new config key for environment-based override, add a row here — no
# entry-point script changes required. Coercion is driven by the type
# of the existing default, so users can keep writing plain strings in
# their .env file.
_ENV_OVERRIDES = {
    "TRADINGAGENTS_LLM_PROVIDER":         "llm_provider",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_DEEP_THINK_LLM":       "deep_think_llm",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_QUICK_THINK_LLM":      "quick_think_llm",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_DEEP_THINK_PROVIDER":       "deep_think_provider",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_QUICK_THINK_PROVIDER":      "quick_think_provider",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_DEEP_THINK_BACKEND_URL":    "deep_think_backend_url",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_QUICK_THINK_BACKEND_URL":   "quick_think_backend_url",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_LLM_BACKEND_URL":      "backend_url",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_OUTPUT_LANGUAGE":      "output_language",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_MAX_DEBATE_ROUNDS":    "max_debate_rounds",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_MAX_RISK_ROUNDS":      "max_risk_discuss_rounds",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS":      "max_tool_rounds",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_CHECKPOINT_ENABLED":   "checkpoint_enabled",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_BENCHMARK_TICKER":     "benchmark_ticker",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_TEMPERATURE":          "temperature",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_LLM_MAX_RETRIES":      "llm_max_retries",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_MAX_TOKENS":           "max_tokens",
    # Provider-specific reasoning/thinking knobs (None = each provider's own
    # default). Settable here for non-interactive runs; the CLI also offers an
    # interactive choice, which is skipped when the matching var is set.
    "TRADINGAGENTS_GOOGLE_THINKING_LEVEL":   "google_thinking_level",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_OPENAI_REASONING_EFFORT": "openai_reasoning_effort",
    "TRADINGAGENTS_ANTHROPIC_EFFORT":        "anthropic_effort",
}


_BOOL_TRUE = ("true", "1", "yes", "on")
_BOOL_FALSE = ("false", "0", "no", "off")


def _coerce(value: str, reference):
    """Coerce env-var string to the type of the existing default value.

    Invalid values raise ``ValueError`` rather than silently falling back to a
    default — a misspelled boolean (e.g. ``treu``) or non-numeric int should fail
    loudly at startup, not quietly misconfigure an unattended run.
    """
    if isinstance(reference, bool):
        normalized = value.strip().lower()
        if normalized in _BOOL_TRUE:
            return True
        if normalized in _BOOL_FALSE:
            return False
        raise ValueError(
            f"expected a boolean ({'/'.join(_BOOL_TRUE + _BOOL_FALSE)}), got {value!r}"
        )
    if isinstance(reference, int) and not isinstance(reference, bool):
        return int(value)
    if isinstance(reference, float):
        return float(value)

def _apply_env_overrides(config: dict) -> dict:
    """Apply TRADINGAGENTS_* env vars to the config dict in-place."""
    for env_var, key in _ENV_OVERRIDES.items():
        raw = os.environ.get(env_var)
        if raw is None or raw == "":
            continue
        try:
            config[key] = _coerce(raw, config.get(key))
        except ValueError as exc:
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid value for {env_var}: {exc}") from exc
    return config


def build_default_config() -> dict:
    """The built-in defaults with the TRADINGAGENTS_* environment folded in.

    Read when the package is imported, as DEFAULT_CONFIG; call it again to see
    the environment as it is now.
    """
    return _apply_env_overrides({
        "results_dir": os.getenv("TRADINGAGENTS_RESULTS_DIR") or os.path.join(_TRADINGAGENTS_HOME, "logs"),
        "data_cache_dir": os.getenv("TRADINGAGENTS_CACHE_DIR") or os.path.join(_TRADINGAGENTS_HOME, "cache"),
        "memory_log_path": os.getenv("TRADINGAGENTS_MEMORY_LOG_PATH") or os.path.join(_TRADINGAGENTS_HOME, "memory", "trading_memory.md"),
        # Optional cap on the number of resolved memory log entries. When set,
        # the oldest resolved entries are pruned once this limit is exceeded.
        # Pending entries are never pruned. None disables rotation entirely.
        "memory_log_max_entries": None,
        # LLM settings
        "llm_provider": "openai",
        "deep_think_llm": "gpt-6-sol",
        "quick_think_llm": "gpt-6-luna",
        # When None, each provider's client falls back to its own default endpoint
        # (api.openai.com for OpenAI, generativelanguage.googleapis.com for Gemini, ...).
        # The CLI overrides this per provider when the user picks one. Keeping a
        # provider-specific URL here would leak (e.g. OpenAI's /v1 was previously
        # being forwarded to Gemini, producing malformed request URLs).
        "backend_url": None,
        # A tier may name its own provider and endpoint (#1440): the quick tier serves
        # the analysts, researchers, debaters and trader, the deep tier the managers.
        # None means the tier uses llm_provider and backend_url.
        "quick_think_provider": None,
        "deep_think_provider": None,
        "quick_think_backend_url": None,
        "deep_think_backend_url": None,
        # Provider-specific thinking configuration
        "google_thinking_level": None,      # "high", "minimal", etc.
        "openai_reasoning_effort": None,    # "medium", "high", "low"
        "anthropic_effort": None,           # "high", "medium", "low"
        # Sampling temperature, forwarded to every provider when set. None leaves
        # each provider at its own default. Lower values reduce run-to-run
        # variation on models that honor it; reasoning models largely ignore it
        # and no setting makes LLM output bit-identical across runs (see README).
        "temperature": None,
        # SDK retry budget forwarded to every provider chat client. None leaves each
        # provider/SDK at its own default (usually 2). Raise it to ride out bursty
        # 429 throttling on rate-limited deployments instead of aborting a run (#1091).
        "llm_max_retries": None,
        # Cap on output tokens forwarded to every provider chat client. None leaves
        # each provider at its own default. Set it to bound a model that emits
        # unbounded reasoning/output and hangs or trips a gateway idle timeout
        # (e.g. some deepseek-v4-flash deployments, #1204).
        "max_tokens": None,
        # Checkpoint/resume: when True, LangGraph saves state after each node
        # so a crashed run can resume from the last successful step.
        "checkpoint_enabled": False,
        # Output language for analyst reports and final decision
        # Internal agent debate stays in English for reasoning quality
        "output_language": "English",
        # Debate and discussion settings
        "max_debate_rounds": 1,
        "max_risk_discuss_rounds": 1,
        "max_recur_limit": 100,
        # Rounds of tool calls an analyst may make before it is asked for its report.
        "max_tool_rounds": 20,
        # News / data fetching parameters
        # Increase for longer lookback strategies or to broaden macro coverage;
        # decrease to reduce token usage in agent prompts.
        "news_article_limit": 20,             # max articles per ticker (ticker-news)
        "global_news_article_limit": 10,      # max articles for global/macro news
        "global_news_lookback_days": 7,       # macro news lookback window
        # Search queries used by get_global_news for macro headlines. Extend or
        # replace to broaden geographic / sector coverage.
        "global_news_queries": [
            "Federal Reserve interest rates inflation",
            "S&P 500 earnings GDP economic outlook",
            "geopolitical risk trade war sanctions",
            "ECB Bank of England BOJ central bank policy",
            "oil commodities supply chain energy",
        ],
        # Data vendor configuration
        # Category-level configuration (default for all tools in category).
        # The configured value is the exact vendor chain — requests are NOT silently
        # routed to vendors you didn't choose. For ordered fallback, list several,
        # e.g. "yfinance,alpha_vantage". "default" uses all available vendors.
        "data_vendors": {
            "core_stock_apis": "yfinance",       # Options: alpha_vantage, yfinance

            # Statements come from SEC EDGAR as filed (US filers), then Yahoo; the

        "fundamental_data": "sec_edgar,yfinance",  # Options: sec_edgar, alpha_vantage, yfinance

            "prediction_markets": "polymarket",  # Options: polymarket (keyless)
        },
        # Tool-level configuration (takes precedence over category-level)
        "tool_vendors": {
            # Example: "get_stock_data": "alpha_vantage",  # Override category default
        },
        # Benchmark for alpha calculation in the reflection layer.
        # ``benchmark_ticker`` (when set) overrides the suffix map for all
        # tickers; leave it None to use ``benchmark_map`` for auto-detection
        # based on the ticker's exchange suffix. SPY remains the US default

        # while non-US tickers get their regional index automatically.
        # Trading days from the first close after the analysis date over which a
        # decision's outcome is measured, for reflection and for the backtest figures.
        "holding_period_days": 5,

"""Tests for TRADINGAGENTS_* env-var overlay onto DEFAULT_CONFIG."""

from __future__ import annotations

import os

import pytest

import tradingagents.default_config as default_config_module


def _config_with_env(monkeypatch, **overrides):
    """The defaults as the environment given here would set them."""
    for key in list(default_config_module._ENV_OVERRIDES):
        monkeypatch.delenv(key, raising=False)
    for key, val in overrides.items():
        monkeypatch.setenv(key, val)
    return default_config_module.build_default_config()


def test_no_env_uses_built_in_defaults(monkeypatch):
    config = _config_with_env(monkeypatch)
    assert config["llm_provider"] == "openai"
    assert config["deep_think_llm"] == "gpt-6-sol"
    assert config["quick_think_llm"] == "gpt-6-luna"
    assert config["backend_url"] is None
    assert config["max_debate_rounds"] == 1
    assert config["checkpoint_enabled"] is False


def test_string_overrides(monkeypatch):
    config = _config_with_env(
        monkeypatch,
        TRADINGAGENTS_LLM_PROVIDER="google",
        TRADINGAGENTS_DEEP_THINK_LLM="gemini-3-pro-preview",
        TRADINGAGENTS_QUICK_THINK_LLM="gemini-3-flash-preview",
        TRADINGAGENTS_LLM_BACKEND_URL="https://example.invalid/v1",
        TRADINGAGENTS_OUTPUT_LANGUAGE="Chinese",
    )
    assert config["llm_provider"] == "google"
    assert config["deep_think_llm"] == "gemini-3-pro-preview"
    assert config["quick_think_llm"] == "gemini-3-flash-preview"
    assert config["backend_url"] == "https://example.invalid/v1"
    assert config["output_language"] == "Chinese"


def test_int_coercion(monkeypatch):
    config = _config_with_env(
        monkeypatch,
        TRADINGAGENTS_MAX_DEBATE_ROUNDS="3",
        TRADINGAGENTS_MAX_RISK_ROUNDS="2",
    )
    assert config["max_debate_rounds"] == 3

"""The CLI must use the memory log the same way propagate() does.

The CLI streams the graph itself instead of calling propagate(), so memory steps
that lived only in propagate() never ran on the primary entry point: pending
decisions were not settled, the Portfolio Manager got no past context, and the
finished decision was not recorded. Both paths now build their initial state and
record their decision through the same graph methods.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

import cli.run as cli_run
from tradingagents.graph.trading_graph import TradingAgentsGraph
from tradingagents.memory import TradingMemoryLog


def _bare_graph(tmp_path):
    """A graph without __init__ (no LLM clients), wired to a temp log."""
    graph = object.__new__(TradingAgentsGraph)
    graph.config = {"memory_log_path": str(tmp_path / "trading_memory.md")}
    graph.memory_log = TradingMemoryLog(graph.config)
    graph._log_state = lambda *a: None   # these tests are about the memory log
    return graph


@pytest.mark.unit
def test_record_decision_appends_a_pending_entry(tmp_path):
    graph = _bare_graph(tmp_path)
    graph.record_decision("NVDA", "2026-01-10", {"final_trade_decision": "Rating: Buy\n\nBuy NVDA."})
    entries = graph.memory_log.load_entries()
    assert [(e["ticker"], e["pending"], e["rating"]) for e in entries] == [("NVDA", True, "Buy")]


@pytest.mark.unit
def test_record_decision_skips_a_run_without_a_decision(tmp_path):
    graph = _bare_graph(tmp_path)
    graph.record_decision("NVDA", "2026-01-10", {})
    assert graph.memory_log.load_entries() == []

## 4. Deferred — fail-closed is not activated in this slice

`record_decision` and the run path are **not** wired to downgrade a run. The
checker is implemented and tested offline, and `material_claims` / `claim_audit`
are declared in `AgentState`. A decision without claims reports

"""Shared helpers for invoking an agent with structured output and a graceful fallback.

The Portfolio Manager, Trader, and Research Manager all follow the same
canonical pattern:

1. At agent creation, wrap the LLM with ``with_structured_output(Schema)``
   so the model returns a typed Pydantic instance. If the provider does
   not support structured output (rare; mostly older Ollama models), the
   wrap is skipped and the agent uses free-text generation instead.
2. At invocation, run the structured call and render the result back to
   markdown. If the structured call itself fails for any reason
   (malformed JSON from a weak model, transient provider issue), fall
   back to a plain ``llm.invoke`` so the pipeline never blocks.

Centralising the pattern here keeps the agent factories small and ensures
all three agents log the same warnings when fallback fires.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from collections.abc import Callable
from typing import Any, TypeVar

from pydantic import BaseModel

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

T = TypeVar("T", bound=BaseModel)

# Schema-only structured output binds exactly one tool (the schema itself), so a
# model that reaches for a search tool emits an unknown tool call and the whole
# structured attempt is discarded for a free-text retry. Agents on this path
# state the constraint explicitly rather than relying on the binding alone
# (#1130).
NO_EXTERNAL_TOOLS = (
    "Use only the evidence provided in this prompt. Do not call external tools "
    "or search the web; if something is missing, say so explicitly."
)


def bind_structured(llm: Any, schema: type[T], agent_name: str) -> Any | None:
    """Return ``llm.with_structured_output(schema)`` or ``None`` if unsupported.

    Logs a warning when the binding fails so the user understands the agent
    will use free-text generation for every call instead of one-shot fallback.
    """
    try:
        return llm.with_structured_output(schema)
    except (NotImplementedError, AttributeError) as exc:
        logger.warning(
            "%s: provider does not support with_structured_output (%s); "
            "falling back to free-text generation",
            agent_name, exc,
        )
        return None


def invoke_structured(structured_llm: Any | None, prompt: Any, agent_name: str) -> T | None:
    """Run the structured call; ``None`` when there is none or it fails.

    ``prompt`` is whatever the underlying LLM accepts (a string for chat
    invocations, a list of message dicts for chat models that take that
    shape), so a caller can forward the same value to its free-text fallback.
    """
    if structured_llm is None:
        return None
    try:
        result = structured_llm.invoke(prompt)
        if result is None:
            # A thinking model can answer in plain text instead of calling
            # the tool, leaving the parser with nothing to return. Treat it
            # as a structured miss and fall back, with a clear reason.
            raise ValueError("structured output returned no parsed result")
        return result
    except Exception as exc:
        logger.warning(
            "%s: structured-output invocation failed (%s); retrying once as free text",
            agent_name, exc,
        )
        return None


def invoke_structured_or_freetext(
    structured_llm: Any | None,
    plain_llm: Any,
    prompt: Any,
    render: Callable[[T], str],
    agent_name: str,
) -> str:
    """Run the structured call and render to markdown; fall back to free-text on any failure."""
    result = invoke_structured(structured_llm, prompt, agent_name)
    if result is not None:
        return render(result)
    return plain_llm.invoke(prompt).content

```

def test_invoke_structured_falls_back_when_result_is_none():
    # A thinking model can answer in plain text, leaving the parser with None.

    from tradingagents.agents.structured import invoke_structured_or_freetext

    structured = MagicMock()
    structured.invoke.return_value = None
    plain = MagicMock()
    plain.invoke.return_value = MagicMock(content="FREETEXT")

    out = invoke_structured_or_freetext(
        structured, plain, "prompt", render=lambda r: r.rating, agent_name="t"
    )
    assert out == "FREETEXT"
    plain.invoke.assert_called_once()

@pytest.mark.unit
def test_claim_enforcement_defaults_false_and_is_env_configurable(monkeypatch):
    assert DEFAULT_CONFIG["claim_enforcement"] is False

    monkeypatch.delenv("TRADINGAGENTS_CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT", raising=False)
    assert build_default_config()["claim_enforcement"] is False

    monkeypatch.setenv("TRADINGAGENTS_CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT", "true")
    assert build_default_config()["claim_enforcement"] is True


@pytest.mark.unit
def test_a_downgraded_audit_does_not_change_the_rating_or_memory_log(tmp_path):
    """claim_enforcement is False, so the audit is recorded without fail-closed."""
    state = _state(material_claims=[
        {"kind": "supporting", "text": "Close was 181.2.",
         "source_id": "podcast_transcript", "observation_date": TRADE_DATE},
    ])
    graph = _graph(tmp_path)
    graph.record_decision("NVDA", TRADE_DATE, state)

    assert graph.config["claim_enforcement"] is False
    assert state["final_rating"] == "Hold"
    assert _logged(tmp_path)["final_rating"] == "Hold"
    entries = graph.memory_log.load_entries()
    assert [(entry["ticker"], entry["rating"]) for entry in entries] == [("NVDA", "Hold")]

```python
def invoke_structured(...):
    if structured_llm is None:
        return None
    for attempt in range(2):
        try:
            result = structured_llm.invoke(prompt)
            if result is None:
                raise ValueError("structured output returned no parsed result")
            return result
        except Exception as exc:
            if attempt == 0:
                logger.warning(
                    "%s: structured-output invocation failed (%s); retrying the structured call once",
                    agent_name, exc,
                )
                continue
            logger.warning(
                "%s: structured-output invocation failed (%s); retrying once as free text",
                agent_name, exc,
            )
            return None
```

def audit_material_claims(final_state: dict) -> dict:
    """Check that every material claim resolves to a report artifact (ADR-001).

    Pure: no I/O and no LLM. Implements REQ-TA-FR-005, REQ-TA-QR-002 and
    REQ-TA-INV-005. A run with no claim metadata is ``NOT_ASSESSED``, never
    ``ACCEPTED``; this slice does not downgrade the run itself.
    """
    items = _claim_items(final_state)
    if not items:
        return {
            "status": "NOT_ASSESSED",
            "reasons": ["NO_CLAIM_METADATA"],
            "claims_total": 0,
            "claims_attributed": 0,
            "unsupported_claim_ids": [],
            "claims": [],
        }


```

`_log_state`, and the state log carries `claim_audit` (status, reasons, counts,
per-claim resolution). The checker is also tested offline, and `material_claims`
/ `claim_audit` are declared in `AgentState`. A decision without claims reports
`NOT_ASSESSED` (never `ACCEPTED`).

Run acceptance is still **not** gated on the audit. `claim_enforcement` is a
reserved boolean (default `False`, env `TRADINGAGENTS_CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT`); no
behaviour branches on it yet. A `DOWNGRADED` or `NOT_ASSESSED` audit does not
change `final_rating`, the memory-log entry, or report rendering. Activating
fail-closed — gating run acceptance on the audit and mapping the status onto the
REQ-TA-FR-006 run-status enum — is a later change.

def test_record_decision_writes_an_accepted_claim_audit(tmp_path):

def _invoke_json_mode(llm, prompt) -> PortfolioDecision | None:
    """Retry the PM call with json_mode before free text.

    deepseek-flash can miss the function-calling path but supports json_mode.
    """
    try:
        structured_json = llm.with_structured_output(PortfolioDecision, method="json_mode")
    except (NotImplementedError, AttributeError) as exc:
        logger.warning("PM: json_mode retry failed (binding: %s)", exc)
        return None
    try:
        result = structured_json.invoke(prompt)
        if result is None:
            raise ValueError("json_mode returned no result")
        return PortfolioDecision.model_validate_json(result)
    except Exception as exc:
        logger.warning("PM: json_mode retry failed (%s)", exc)
        return None
```

def _price_df(prices, start="2026-01-05"):
    """Minimal DataFrame matching yfinance .history() output shape.

    Uses a DatetimeIndex like real yfinance output, so resolution-date
    extraction (stock.index[holding_days]) works (#1251).
    """
    idx = pd.date_range(start=start, periods=len(prices), freq="D")
    return pd.DataFrame({"Close": prices}, index=idx)


def _make_pm_state(past_context=""):
    """Minimal AgentState dict for portfolio_manager_node."""
    return {
        "company_of_interest": "NVDA",
        "past_context": past_context,
        "risk_debate_state": {
            "history": "Risk debate history.",
            "aggressive_history": "",
            "conservative_history": "",
            "neutral_history": "",
            "current_aggressive_response": "",
            "current_conservative_response": "",
            "current_neutral_response": "",
            "count": 1,
        },
        "market_report": "Market report.",
        "sentiment_report": "Sentiment report.",
        "news_report": "News report.",
        "fundamentals_report": "Fundamentals report.",
        "investment_plan": "Research plan.",
        "trader_investment_plan": "Trader plan.",
    }


def _structured_pm_llm(captured: dict, decision: PortfolioDecision | None = None):
    """Build a MagicMock LLM whose with_structured_output binding captures the
    prompt and returns a real PortfolioDecision (so render_pm_decision works).
    """
    if decision is None:
        decision = PortfolioDecision(
            rating=PortfolioRating.HOLD,
            executive_summary="Hold the position; await catalyst.",
            investment_thesis="Balanced view; neither side carried the debate.",
        )
    structured = MagicMock()
    structured.invoke.side_effect = lambda prompt: (
        captured.__setitem__("prompt", prompt) or decision
    )
    llm = MagicMock()
    llm.with_structured_output.return_value = structured
    return llm

    def test_pm_returns_rendered_markdown_with_rating(self):
        """The structured PortfolioDecision is rendered to markdown that
        downstream consumers (memory log, signal processor, CLI display)
        can parse without any extra LLM call."""
        captured = {}
        decision = PortfolioDecision(
            rating=PortfolioRating.OVERWEIGHT,
            executive_summary="Build position gradually over the next two weeks.",
            investment_thesis="AI capex cycle remains intact; institutional flows constructive.",
            price_target=215.0,
            time_horizon="3-6 months",
        )
        llm = _structured_pm_llm(captured, decision)
        pm_node = create_portfolio_manager(llm)
        result = pm_node(_make_pm_state())
        md = result["final_trade_decision"]
        assert "**Rating**: Overweight" in md
        assert "**Executive Summary**: Build position gradually" in md
        assert "**Investment Thesis**: AI capex cycle" in md
        assert "**Price Target**: 215.0" in md
        assert "**Time Horizon**: 3-6 months" in md

    def test_pm_falls_back_to_freetext_when_structured_unavailable(self):
        """If a provider does not support with_structured_output, the agent
        falls back to a plain invoke and returns whatever prose the model
        produced, so the pipeline never blocks."""
        plain_response = "**Rating**: Sell\n\nExit ahead of guidance."
        llm = MagicMock()
        llm.with_structured_output.side_effect = NotImplementedError("provider unsupported")
        llm.invoke.return_value = MagicMock(content=plain_response)
        pm_node = create_portfolio_manager(llm)
        result = pm_node(_make_pm_state())
        assert result["final_trade_decision"] == plain_response

"""Portfolio Manager: synthesises the risk-analyst debate into the final decision.

Uses LangChain's ``with_structured_output`` so the LLM produces a typed
``PortfolioDecision`` directly, in a single call. Its rating is the run's
``final_rating``, and the decision is rendered to markdown as
``final_trade_decision`` for the memory log, CLI display and saved reports.
The node also returns ``material_claims``: the structured supporting and
opposing factors, each linked to a report artifact (ADR-001).
When a provider does not expose structured output, the agent falls back to
free-text generation and the rating is read from that text.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from tradingagents.agents.context import (
    get_instrument_context_from_state,
    get_language_instruction,
    get_portfolio_context_from_state,
)
from tradingagents.agents.rating import parse_rating
from tradingagents.agents.schemas import (
    CLAIM_SOURCE_IDS,
    PortfolioDecision,
    render_pm_decision,
)
from tradingagents.agents.structured import NO_EXTERNAL_TOOLS, bind_structured, invoke_structured


def create_portfolio_manager(llm):
    structured_llm = bind_structured(llm, PortfolioDecision, "Portfolio Manager")

    def portfolio_manager_node(state) -> dict:
        instrument_context = get_instrument_context_from_state(state)
        portfolio_context = get_portfolio_context_from_state(state)

---
from: {CLAIM_SOURCE_IDS}. Give `observation_date` as YYYY-MM-DD whenever the
claim asserts market data (a price or indicator); omit it for qualitative
claims. Do not name the producing agent: it is derived from the source.

{NO_EXTERNAL_TOOLS}{get_language_instruction()}"""

        # The typed rating is the decision; the rendered text only carries it.
        # Read back from text, a rating the thesis quotes could replace it.
        decision = invoke_structured(structured_llm, prompt, "Portfolio Manager")
        claims = []
        if decision is not None:
            final_trade_decision = render_pm_decision(decision)
            final_rating = decision.rating.value
            # ADR-001: a material claim is exactly an item in these two lists.
            claims = [
                {
                    "kind": kind,
                    "text": claim.text,
                    "source_id": claim.source_id,
                    "observation_date": claim.observation_date,
                }
                for kind, factors in (
                    ("supporting", decision.supporting_factors),
                    ("opposing", decision.opposing_factors),
                )
                for claim in factors
            ]
        else:
            final_trade_decision = llm.invoke(prompt).content
            final_rating = parse_rating(final_trade_decision)

        new_risk_debate_state = {
            "history": risk_debate_state["history"],
            "aggressive_history": risk_debate_state["aggressive_history"],
            "conservative_history": risk_debate_state["conservative_history"],
            "neutral_history": risk_debate_state["neutral_history"],
            "latest_speaker": "Judge",
            "current_aggressive_response": risk_debate_state["current_aggressive_response"],
            "current_conservative_response": risk_debate_state["current_conservative_response"],
            "current_neutral_response": risk_debate_state["current_neutral_response"],
            "count": risk_debate_state["count"],
        }

        return {
            "risk_debate_state": new_risk_debate_state,
            "final_trade_decision": final_trade_decision,
            "final_rating": final_rating,
            "material_claims": claims,
            # ADR-001: makes a structured-output miss visible to the audit,
            # which otherwise cannot tell it from a model that emitted no claims.
            "claim_fallback": None if decision is not None else "free_text",
        }

    return portfolio_manager_node

```

Detailed code:

```python
import logging

from tradingagents.agents.schemas import ...

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def _invoke_json_mode(llm, prompt) -> PortfolioDecision | None:
    """Retry the PM decision as JSON, for providers that miss function calling.

    ADR-001: a json_mode decision is a structured decision, so the claims/rating
    path is unchanged and ``claim_fallback`` stays None.
    """
    try:
        structured_json = llm.with_structured_output(PortfolioDecision, method="json_mode")
    except (NotImplementedError, AttributeError) as exc:
        logger.warning("PM: json_mode retry failed (binding: %s)", exc)
        return None
    try:
        result = structured_json.invoke(prompt)
        if result is None:
            raise ValueError("json_mode returned no result")
        if isinstance(result, PortfolioDecision):
            return result
        return PortfolioDecision.model_validate_json(result)
    except Exception as exc:
        logger.warning("PM: json_mode retry failed (%s)", exc)
        return None

class PortfolioDecision(BaseModel):
    """Structured output produced by the Portfolio Manager.

    The model fills every field as part of its primary LLM call; no separate
    extraction pass is required. Field descriptions double as the model's
    output instructions, so the prompt body only needs to convey context and
    the rating-scale guidance.
    """

    rating: PortfolioRating = Field(
        description=(
            "The final position rating. Exactly one of Buy / Overweight / Hold / "
            "Underweight / Sell, picked based on the analysts' debate. "
            "Conflicting arguments alone are not a reason to Hold: commit to the "
            "stronger side, sized by how decisively it wins. Choose Hold only "
            "when the evidence is still balanced after weighing, or too thin to "
            "support a call."
        ),
    )
    executive_summary: str = Field(
        description=(
            "A concise action plan covering entry strategy, position sizing, "
            "key risk levels, and time horizon. Two to four sentences."
        ),
    )
    investment_thesis: str = Field(
        description=(
            "Detailed reasoning anchored in specific evidence from the analysts' "
            "debate. If prior lessons are referenced in the prompt context, "
            "incorporate them; otherwise rely solely on the current analysis."
        ),
    )
    supporting_factors: list[MaterialClaim] = Field(
        default_factory=list,
        description=(
            "Exactly three material claims that support the rating, each linked to "
            "the report artifact it came from (ADR-001)."
        ),
    )
    opposing_factors: list[MaterialClaim] = Field(
        default_factory=list,
        description=(
            "Exactly three material claims that argue against the rating, each "
            "linked to the report artifact it came from (ADR-001)."
        ),
    )
    price_target: float | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Optional target price in the instrument's quote currency.",
    )
    time_horizon: str | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Optional recommended holding period, e.g. '3-6 months'.",
    )

    @field_validator("price_target", mode="before")
    @classmethod
    def _nullish_float_to_none(cls, v):
        return _coerce_optional_float(v)


def render_pm_decision(decision: PortfolioDecision) -> str:
    """Render a PortfolioDecision back to the markdown shape the rest of the system expects.

    Memory log, CLI display, and saved report files all read this markdown,
    so the rendered output preserves the exact section headers (``**Rating**``,
    ``**Executive Summary**``, ``**Investment Thesis**``) that downstream
    parsers and the report writers already handle.

===

    Material claims render as two extra labelled lists (ADR-001); they are
    omitted when the model returned none, so an older state renders unchanged.
    """
    parts = [
        f"**Rating**: {decision.rating.value}",
        "",
        f"**Executive Summary**: {decision.executive_summary}",
        "",
        f"**Investment Thesis**: {decision.investment_thesis}",
    ]
    for label, claims in (("Supporting Factors", decision.supporting_factors),
                          ("Opposing Factors", decision.opposing_factors)):
        if not claims:
            continue
        parts.extend(["", f"**{label}**:"])
        for claim in claims:
            source = f"source: {claim.source_id}"
            if claim.observation_date:
                source += f", observation: {claim.observation_date}"
            parts.append(f"- {claim.text} ({source})")
    # Named even when absent: a missing line reads as a field nobody asked for,
    # so a reader cannot tell "no target" from "target not reported".
    target = decision.price_target if decision.price_target is not None else "not provided"
    parts.extend(["", f"**Price Target**: {target}"])
    parts.extend(["", f"**Time Horizon**: {decision.time_horizon or 'not provided'}"])
    return "\n".join(parts)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Sentiment Analyst
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


class SentimentBand(StrEnum):
    """Discrete sentiment direction produced by the Sentiment Analyst.

    Six tiers keep the signal granular enough to be actionable while remaining
    small enough for every provider to map reliably from its JSON output.
    """

    def test_percentage_answer_to_a_price_field_becomes_none(self):
        # The Trader is asked for concrete levels and may answer a price field
        # with a distance ("15%"), which failed the whole proposal (#1288).
        # A percentage cannot be salvaged: 15% must not become a $15 stop.
        for pct in ("15%", " 7.5% ", "-10%"):
            p = TraderProposal(
                action=TraderAction.BUY,
                reasoning="x",
                entry_price=pct,
                stop_loss=pct,
            )
            assert p.entry_price is None
            assert p.stop_loss is None

    def test_human_formatted_price_is_reduced_to_its_number(self):
        p = TraderProposal(
            action=TraderAction.BUY,
            reasoning="x",
            entry_price="$1,234.50",
            stop_loss="1,180",
        )
        assert p.entry_price == 1234.50
        assert p.stop_loss == 1180.0

    def test_one_bad_field_no_longer_fails_the_whole_proposal(self):
        # Previously a single '15%' raised, forcing a free-text retry that lost
        # the action and reasoning; now the rest of the proposal survives.

1: # AGENTS.md — coding-agent entry point (deliverable D3)
2: 
3: This is the single entry point every AI coding agent reads before doing any
4: work in this repository. Human team members: review changes to this file like
5: changes to a contract — it constrains what agents may do (project.pdf p5 D3).
6: 
7: ## 1. Build target
8: 
9: Enhance the TradingAgents stock-analysis agent (upstream v0.7.1 baseline) with
10: a set of agreed functional and non-functional requirements under the CS5351
11: agentic-SE rules: requirements are machine-readable, architecture artifacts
12: constrain the agent, all code is generated under human supervision, and every
13: claim is traceable. The authoritative requirement set is
14: `docs/req/requirements.yaml`.
15: 
16: ## 2. Reading order (mandatory, before any plan or code)
17: 
18: 1. `AGENTS.md` (this file)
19: 2. `Prompt.md` — the launch workflow: plan first, human approval, staged build
20: 3. `docs/req/requirements.yaml` — authoritative registry (schema-gated)
21: 4. `docs/req/requirement-candidates.md` — non-authoritative brainstorm pool
22:    (background only; never treat it as committed scope)
23: 5. `docs/architecture/` — blueprint, ADRs, invariants, trace matrix
24:    (artifacts land in S2; until then this directory may be partial — do not
25:    invent missing architecture, ask the human)
26: 6. `docs/code-governance/` — structural constraints, verification constraints,
27:    tool manifest
28: 7. `.agent/rules.md`, `.opencode/rules.md`, `.opencode/permissions.jsonc`
29: 
30: ## 3. Code baseline
31: 
32: Upstream release v0.7.1 (merged as PR #1) plus the src-layout reorganisation
33: (PR #2). New work **extends** this baseline; it never replaces baseline
34: behaviour silently. Removing or changing existing baseline behaviour requires
35: an ADR and human approval (see `.agent/rules.md` once authored).
36: 
37: ## 4. Editable / review-required / frozen zones
38: 
39: | Zone | Status for agents |
40: |---|---|
41: | `src/**`, `test/**` | editable, subject to §7 verification requirements and the test-approval gate |
42: | `prompts/**` | editable; any change triggers the semantic evaluation gate once it exists (DEBT-003) |
43: | `docs/**`, `tools/ci/**`, `.github/**` | editable for governance work, but workflow changes require human review in the PR |
44: | `.opencode/rules.md` | **human-only** — agents must never write it (project.pdf p6) |
45: | `.env`, any secret-bearing file | **never read, never write, never echo** |
46: | `CHANGELOG.md` released sections, `LICENSE`, upstream release workflows | frozen (append-only for CHANGELOG's Unreleased section) |
47: 
48: ## 5. Module organisation
49: 
50: ```
51: src/tradingagents/   agents/ (analysts, researchers, managers, trader, risk_mgmt;
52:                      structured.py, rating.py, schemas.py, state.py, context.py)
53:                      graph/ (setup, trading_graph, conditional_logic, propagation,
54:                      analyst_execution, checkpointer)
55:                      dataflows/ (vendors, date_window, errors, net, symbols, ...)
56:                      memory/  llm_clients/  default_config.py  reporting.py
57:                      report_html.py  backtest.py  portfolio.py
58: src/cli/             main, run, stats_handler, display, prefs, models, ...
59: ```
60: 
61: One architectural element maps to exactly one module
62: (`docs/code-governance/structural-constraints.md`); the element→module mapping
63: table is finalised with the architecture blueprint (S2).
64: 
65: ## 6. Integration contracts between modules
66: 
67: To be fixed when the first cross-requirement slices start (planned: token
68: accounting exposed from `cli/stats_handler.py` to graph callers; the
69: profile→settings mapping; the shared evidence-citation format for FR-03/FR-05).
70: Until an interface is written here, agents must not invent cross-module APIs —
71: propose them in the execution plan and wait for approval.
72: 
73: ## 7. Verification requirements for generated code
74: 
75: - `ruff check .` passes (strict, full repo)
76: - the full offline suite passes (`pytest -q`, network-free, zero regressions
77:   against the previous round)
78: - every touched requirement with status `implement` has a test matching its
79:   `fit_criterion`; every invariant has a negative test that fails on violation
80: - tests cite the requirement/invariant ID they verify
81: - live/API-dependent tests go under `test/live/` and are marked `integration`
82: - registry changes must pass the schema gate (`docs/req/req-schema.json`)
83: - every agent session behind a PR is recorded in `docs/ai-use-log.yaml`
84:   (gate 6), once that file exists at the team's formal kickoff
85: 
86: ## 8. Working rules
87: 
88: - Start from `Prompt.md`; never run unattended end-to-end; stop at every human
89:   gate.
90: - Don't invent thresholds: where the registry says `TBD`, say `TBD` and run the
91:   baseline experiment instead of guessing a number.
92: - Record interventions: when a human redirects/approves/rejects your work,
93:   that belongs in the session transcript and the AI-use log entry.
94: 
95: ## 9. Branch model (SCM)
96: 
97: | Branch | Role | How changes land |
98: |---|---|---|
99: | `main` | protected mainline | **PRs only** — CI must pass + human review (branch protection). Two kinds of PRs land here: periodic batch merges from `integration`, and important updates merged directly. |
100: | `integration` | working integration branch | exists to **reduce manual PR count**: routine work is pushed directly (or via PR when the author wants review). Periodically batch-merged back to `main` via PR; immediately after `main` changes, `main` is pulled back into `integration`. |
101: | `feat/**`, `gates/**`, `docs/**` | optional feature branches | for risky or reviewable work; merged into `integration` (PR or direct), following the `integration` rules above. |
102: 
103: Consequences for agents:
104: 
105: - After any `main` change (batch or direct), run/fetch `git pull origin
106:   integration` state — `integration` is rebased on `main` right away; never
107:   let the two diverge for long.
108: - PR-triggered course gates (test approval, AI-use log) fire on **every PR**,
109:   so they bite at each batch merge into `main` and on any review-wanted PR
110:   into `integration`. Push-triggered CI on `integration` continuously covers
111:   schema, lint, tests, and the advisory audit.
112: - The submission zip is cut from `main` (after the final batch merge).

1: # Prompt.md — static launch prompt (deliverable D9)
2: 
3: You are the AI coding agent for this repository. `AGENTS.md` is your entry
4: point; after reading it and its reading order, follow exactly this workflow.
5: This prompt is a launch instruction only — it does not authorise an unattended
6: end-to-end run (project.pdf p7 D9).
7: 
8: ## Step 1 — Understand
9: 
10: - Read the `AGENTS.md` reading order in full.
11: - Restate the task in your own words, citing the requirement IDs
12:   (`REQ-TA-...`) it touches. If the task maps to no registry entry, or to an
13:   entry whose status is `candidate`, stop and ask the human; do not invent
14:   requirements or thresholds.
15: 
16: ## Step 2 — Plan (before any code)
17: 
18: Produce an execution plan containing:
19: 
20: 1. **Traceability** — requirement/invariant IDs ↔ planned steps ↔ files
21: 2. **Interface contracts** — signatures/behaviours of anything crossing module
22:    boundaries (see AGENTS.md §6; propose, don't assume)
23: 3. **Scope boundaries** — files you will create/modify, and files you
24:    explicitly will not touch
25: 4. **Testing strategy** — the offline test per fit criterion, the negative
26:    test per invariant, and how the full previous suite stays green
27: 5. **Steps** — ordered, each small enough to verify independently
28: 
29: ## Step 3 — STOP and wait
30: 
31: Present the plan and **stop**. Do not write, modify, or delete any code until
32: the human explicitly approves the plan. Log this approval point; it becomes an
33: annotated intervention in the session transcript.
34: 
35: ## Step 4 — Build in stages (after approval)
36: 
37: - Implement one stage at a time, extending the baseline, never replacing it.
38: - After each stage: run `ruff check .` and the offline suite; report results
39:   before moving on. The human gates each stage transition.
40: - Stay inside the plan's scope boundaries. Needing to exceed them means
41:   stopping and re-planning (back to Step 2), not improvising.
42: 
43: ## Step 5 — Self-verify
44: 
45: - Full offline suite: zero regressions.
46: - Fit-criterion tests and invariant negative tests for the touched
47:   requirements, each citing its ID.
48: - Summarise the evidence for the trace matrix (component, test, monitor).
49: 
50: ## Step 6 — Record the AI-use log (gate 6)
51: 
52: Before you finish any session that changed code, tests, or prompts: create
53: `docs/ai-use-log.yaml` if it does not exist, then append one entry. Required
54: fields are `id`, `date`, `task`; also record `agent`, `model`, `prompt_ref`,
55: `files`, `human_decision`, and `transcript`. Never invent a session. Commit the
56: entry in the same PR as the change; gate 6 blocks otherwise.
57: 
58: ## Hard rules
59: 
60: - Never read, write, or echo secrets (`.env`, API keys).
61: - Never touch frozen zones (AGENTS.md §4).
62: - Where the registry says `TBD`, output `TBD` and propose the baseline
63:   experiment — never invent a number.
64: - Every session that changes code, tests, or prompts gets an entry in
65:   `docs/ai-use-log.yaml` (Step 6; gate 6 enforces it).

1: # ADR-001 — Material claims in the final recommendation
2: 
3: - **Status:** Approved (2026-11-15)
4: - **Deciders:** human reviewer (session approval)
5: - **Traces:** REQ-TA-FR-005, REQ-TA-QR-002, REQ-TA-INV-005
6: - **Context requirements:** REQ-TA-FR-004 and REQ-TA-INV-001 (rating semantics
7:   stay with those entries; this ADR does not change them)
8: - **Scope of this slice:** local vertical slice only. No run-path downgrade is
9:   activated (see §4).
10: 
11: ## 1. Decision — what counts as a material claim
12: 
13: A **material claim** is exactly one item in the final recommendation's
14: structured **supporting-factor list** or **opposing-factor list**. The lists are
15: fixed at **three supporting and three opposing claims** per recommendation.
16: Everything else in the report (analyst reports, research/trading/risk sections,
17: the executive summary and investment-thesis prose) is narrative and is not a
18: material claim for this ADR.
19: 
20: Rationale: QR-002 measures a fraction of *formally defined* claims, and INV-005
21: needs a deterministic set to check. A closed, schema-backed list makes both
22: possible without an NLP classifier. Option A was chosen over rule-based prose
23: detection (unstable boundaries) and over claim-keying every report section
24: (unbounded token and audit cost).
25: 
26: ## 2. Decision — how a claim carries its source
27: 
28: Each claim carries:
29: 
30: | Field | Meaning |
31: |---|---|
32: | `text` | The claim itself, one statement. |
33: | `source_id` | An artifact key from the report's existing `_SECTIONS` table (`market_report`, `sentiment_report`, `news_report`, `fundamentals_report`, `investment_debate_state.bull_history`, `investment_debate_state.bear_history`, `investment_plan`, `trader_investment_plan`, `risk_debate_state.{aggressive,conservative,neutral}_history`). |
34: | `observation_date` | `YYYY-MM-DD`; required when the claim asserts market data (currently the `market_report` source). |
35: 
36: The **producing agent is derived from `source_id`** by the checker using
37: `_SECTIONS`; it is never self-reported by the model. The `source_id` is the
38: identity: aliases, URLs or free-text citations are not accepted. The audit
39: treats `source_id` as resolvable only when the artifact is in the vocabulary
40: **and** the run actually contains a non-empty value at that state path.
41: 
42: The Portfolio Manager fills these fields in its existing structured-output call;
43: there is no extra LLM call and no post-hoc extraction pass.
44: 
45: ## 3. Decision — acceptance rule
46: 
47: The pure checker `audit_material_claims(final_state)` returns a `ClaimAudit`:
48: 
49: - `ACCEPTED` — every material claim resolves to a non-empty artifact, every
50:   market-data claim carries a valid observation date no later than the analysis
51:   date, at most three claims per side, at least one claim cites an analyst
52:   report, and at least one attributed claim carries a dated observation.
53: - `DOWNGRADED` — one or more of those checks fail. Unresolvable claims are
54:   labelled `UNSUPPORTED` and stay visible; nothing is silently dropped. Reason
55:   codes are machine-readable.
56: - `NOT_ASSESSED` / `NO_CLAIM_METADATA` — the run has no claim metadata at all.
57:   This is **not** acceptance.
58: 
59: Reason codes: `NO_CLAIM_METADATA`, `UNTRACEABLE_CLAIM`, `UNKNOWN_SOURCE`,
60: `INVALID_CLAIM`, `CLAIM_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `UNDATED_MARKET_DATA`,
61: `INVALID_OBSERVATION_DATE`, `NO_ANALYST_REFERENCE`, `NO_DATED_OBSERVATION`.
62: 
63: This status is a **report-level traceability status**, not a rating. It does not
64: change `final_rating`, the REVIEW rule (REQ-TA-INV-001) or the validation gate
65: (REQ-TA-FR-004).
66: 
67: ## 4. Deferred — fail-closed is not activated in this slice
68: 
69: The audit is now recorded: `record_decision` runs `audit_material_claims` before
70: `_log_state`, and the state log carries `claim_audit` (status, reasons, counts,
71: per-claim resolution). The checker is also tested offline, and `material_claims`
72: / `claim_audit` are declared in `AgentState`. A decision without claims reports
73: `NOT_ASSESSED` (never `ACCEPTED`).
74: 
75: A structured-output miss is now distinguishable from a model that emitted no
76: claims: the Portfolio Manager records `claim_fallback="free_text"` when the
77: decision came from the plain-text fallback, and the audit then reports
78: `NO_CLAIM_METADATA` followed by `PM_FREETEXT_FALLBACK`. When the structured
79: call succeeded, `claim_fallback` is `None`. The fallback text itself is not
80: parsed for claims.
81: 
82: Run acceptance is still **not** gated on the audit. `claim_enforcement` is a
83: reserved boolean (default `False`, env `TRADINGAGENTS_CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT`); no
84: behaviour branches on it yet. A `DOWNGRADED` or `NOT_ASSESSED` audit does not
85: change `final_rating`, the memory-log entry, or report rendering. Activating
86: fail-closed — gating run acceptance on the audit and mapping the status onto the
87: REQ-TA-FR-006 run-status enum — is a later change.
88: 
89: ## 5. QR-002 — unconditional traceability
90: 
91: The sample was measured on 2026-11-13 (NVDA, 0700.HK, BABA; deepseek-flash for
92: both tiers): 3/3 runs ACCEPTED, 18/18 material claims attributed. The confidence
93: validation run on 2026-11-12 (AAPL, same models) also produced 6/6 ACCEPTED with
94: `confidence = medium`.
95: 
96: Following that evidence, REQ-TA-QR-002 is unconditional: every formally defined
97: material claim in an accepted report must resolve to a report artifact and a
98: derivable producing agent. An untraceable claim makes the report not accepted
99: (REQ-TA-INV-005). No percentage threshold is set; a fraction would allow a
100: report with untraceable claims to pass.
101: 
102: ## 6. Token cost
103: 
104: Claim fields ride in the existing Portfolio Manager structured-output call, so
105: there is no extra call. Added output is bounded by the six claims (text plus one
106: source identifier and, for market data, one date). No numeric token budget is
107: asserted here; token accounting belongs to REQ-TA-COST-001 and must be measured,
108: not assumed.
109: 
110: ## 7. Reopen conditions
111: 
112: 1. The §8 row-3 baseline attribution measurement is recorded → confirm or revise
113:    the accepted-result rule.
114: 2. REQ-TA-FR-003 structured debate lands → decide whether debate evidence becomes
115:    a first-class source type.
116: 3. REQ-TA-FR-006 statuses land → replace the report-level status with the run
117:    status and activate fail-closed through that gate.
118: 4. The free-text Portfolio Manager fallback is exercised in a real run.
119: 5. The sampled human claim audit shows the definition misclassifies principal
120:    reasons → reopen Option A vs prose detection.
121: 6. A measured token-overhead threshold is approved and exceeded.
122: 7. A structured-output provider rejects the added fields.
123: 8. The `_SECTIONS` artifact vocabulary changes.
124: 
125: ## 8. Consequences
126: 
127: - The structured Portfolio Manager call gains two bounded lists; existing
128:   fixtures remain valid because both lists default to empty.
129: - Reports gain an evidence ledger and the "not investment advice" notice.
130: - INV-005 is checkable offline as a pure function; run-level enforcement is
131:   explicitly deferred.

1: """Reusable report-tree writer shared by the CLI and the programmatic API.
2: 
3: Writes a run's per-section markdown (analysts, research, trading, risk,
4: portfolio) plus a consolidated ``complete_report.md`` under ``save_path``. The
5: CLI and ``TradingAgentsGraph.save_reports`` both call this, so a headless / API
6: run produces the same on-disk report tree a CLI run does.
7: """
8: 
9: from datetime import datetime
10: from pathlib import Path
11: 
12: from tradingagents.agents.rating import run_rating
13: 
14: 
15: def analyst_names(keys) -> list[str]:
16:     """The analysts as users select them: the sentiment analyst's key is "social"."""
17:     return ["sentiment" if key == "social" else key for key in keys or []]
18: 
19: 
20: def _header(ticker: str, final_state: dict, settings: dict | None) -> str:
21:     """The report's title and what produced it: analysis date, version, models, analysts, vendors."""
22:     lines = [f"# Trading Analysis Report: {ticker}", ""]
23:     if final_state.get("trade_date"):
24:         lines.append(f"- Analysis date: {final_state['trade_date']}")
25:     if final_state.get("final_trade_decision"):
26:         lines.append(f"- Rating: {run_rating(final_state)}")
27:     traceability = _traceability_header(final_state)
28:     if traceability:
29:         lines.append(traceability)
30:     lines.append(f"- Generated: {datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}")
31:     if settings:
32:         s = settings.get
33:         deep_provider = s("deep_think_provider") or s("llm_provider", "?")
34:         quick_provider = s("quick_think_provider") or s("llm_provider", "?")
35:         if deep_provider == quick_provider:
36:             models = f"{deep_provider}, deep {s('deep_think_llm', '?')}, quick {s('quick_think_llm', '?')}"
37:         else:   # each tier on its own provider (#1440)
38:             models = (f"deep {deep_provider} {s('deep_think_llm', '?')}, "
39:                       f"quick {quick_provider} {s('quick_think_llm', '?')}")
40:         lines.append(f"- TradingAgents {s('version', '?')}: {models}")
41:         lines.append(f"- Analysts: {', '.join(analyst_names(s('analysts')))}; "
42:                      f"research debate rounds {s('max_debate_rounds', '?')}, "
43:                      f"risk debate rounds {s('max_risk_discuss_rounds', '?')}")
44:         vendors = {**(s("data_vendors") or {}), **(s("tool_vendors") or {})}
45:         if vendors:
46:             lines.append("- Data vendors: " + ", ".join(f"{k} {v}" for k, v in vendors.items()))
47:     if final_state.get("memory_note"):
48:         lines.append(f"- Memory log: {final_state['memory_note']}")
49:     return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
50: 
51: 
52: # The material-claim contract fixed by ADR-001: the final recommendation's
53: # supporting and opposing factor lists, this many per side.
54: CLAIMS_PER_SIDE = 3
55: 
56: # The report's own artifacts are the closed source vocabulary. ``source_id``
57: # resolves to (producing agent, state path); the model never names the agent.
58: _ANALYST_SOURCES = frozenset({
59:     "market_report", "sentiment_report", "news_report", "fundamentals_report",
60: })
61: # Claims that assert market data must carry the observation's date.
62: _MARKET_DATA_SOURCES = frozenset({"market_report"})
63: _NOT_A_SOURCE = frozenset({"final_trade_decision"})
64: 
65: # REQ-TA-FR-005: every report ends with this notice, in text and HTML alike.
66: NOT_INVESTMENT_ADVICE = (
67:     "Not investment advice. This report is for research and information purposes "
68:     "only and is not investment, financial, or trading advice."
69: )
70: 
71: 
72: def source_vocabulary() -> dict[str, tuple[str, tuple[str, ...]]]:
73:     """The artifacts a material claim may cite: source_id -> (agent, state path)."""
74:     vocabulary = {}
75:     for _heading, _folder, agents in _SECTIONS:
76:         for agent, _filename, path in agents:
77:             if ".".join(path) in _NOT_A_SOURCE:
78:                 continue
79:             vocabulary[".".join(path)] = (agent, path)
80:     return vocabulary
81: 
82: 
83: def _artifact_value(final_state: dict, path: tuple[str, ...]):
84:     value = final_state
85:     for key in path:
86:         value = value.get(key) if isinstance(value, dict) else None
87:     return value
88: 
89: 
90: def _iso_date(value) -> str | None:
91:     """A canonical ``YYYY-MM-DD`` date, or None for anything else."""
92:     if not isinstance(value, str):
93:         return None
94:     try:
95:         parsed = datetime.strptime(value, "%Y-%m-%d")
96:     except ValueError:
97:         return None
98:     return value if parsed.strftime("%Y-%m-%d") == value else None
99: 
100: 
101: def _claim_items(final_state: dict) -> list[dict]:
102:     """The run's claims in stable order, with deterministic ids (s1..., o1...)."""
103:     raw = final_state.get("material_claims")
104:     if not isinstance(raw, list):
105:         return []
106:     counts = {"supporting": 0, "opposing": 0}
107:     items = []
108:     for entry in raw:
109:         if not isinstance(entry, dict):
110:             continue
111:         declared = entry.get("kind")
112:         kind = declared if declared in counts else "supporting"
113:         counts[kind] += 1
114:         items.append({
115:             "id": f"{kind[0]}{counts[kind]}",
116:             "kind": kind,
117:             "declared_kind": declared,
118:             "text": entry.get("text"),
119:             "source_id": entry.get("source_id"),
120:             "observation_date": entry.get("observation_date"),
121:         })
122:     return items
123: 
124: 
125: def audit_material_claims(final_state: dict) -> dict:
126:     """Check that every material claim resolves to a report artifact (ADR-001).
127: 
128:     Pure: no I/O and no LLM. Implements REQ-TA-FR-005, REQ-TA-QR-002 and
129:     REQ-TA-INV-005. A run with no claim metadata is ``NOT_ASSESSED``, never
130:     ``ACCEPTED``; this slice does not downgrade the run itself.
131:     """
132:     items = _claim_items(final_state)
133:     if not items:
134:         reasons = ["NO_CLAIM_METADATA"]
135:         if final_state.get("claim_fallback") == "free_text":
136:             reasons.append("PM_FREETEXT_FALLBACK")
137:         return {
138:             "status": "NOT_ASSESSED",
139:             "reasons": reasons,
140:             "claims_total": 0,
141:             "claims_attributed": 0,
142:             "unsupported_claim_ids": [],
143:             "claims": [],
144:         }
145: 
146:     vocabulary = source_vocabulary()
147:     trade_date = _iso_date(final_state.get("trade_date"))
148:     reasons: list[str] = []
149:     unsupported: list[str] = []
150:     details: list[dict] = []
151: 
152:     def note(reason: str) -> None:
153:         if reason not in reasons:
154:             reasons.append(reason)
155: 
156:     for item in items:
157:         claim_reasons: list[str] = []
158:         if item["declared_kind"] not in ("supporting", "opposing"):
159:             claim_reasons.append("INVALID_CLAIM")
160:         if not isinstance(item["text"], str) or not item["text"].strip():
161:             claim_reasons.append("UNTRACEABLE_CLAIM")
162: 
163:         source_id = item["source_id"]
164:         producer = None
165:         if not isinstance(source_id, str) or source_id not in vocabulary:
166:             claim_reasons.append("UNKNOWN_SOURCE")
167:         else:
168:             producer, path = vocabulary[source_id]
169:             if not _artifact_value(final_state, path):
170:                 producer = None
171:                 claim_reasons.append("UNKNOWN_SOURCE")
172: 
173:         raw_date = item["observation_date"]
174:         observed = _iso_date(raw_date)
175:         if raw_date not in (None, ""):
176:             if observed is None or (trade_date is not None and observed > trade_date):
177:                 claim_reasons.append("INVALID_OBSERVATION_DATE")
178:         elif source_id in _MARKET_DATA_SOURCES:
179:             claim_reasons.append("UNDATED_MARKET_DATA")
180: 
181:         claim_reasons = list(dict.fromkeys(claim_reasons))
182:         attributed = not claim_reasons
183:         if not attributed:
184:             unsupported.append(item["id"])
185:             for reason in claim_reasons:
186:                 note(reason)
187:         details.append({
188:             "id": item["id"],
189:             "kind": item["kind"],
190:             "text": item["text"],
191:             "source_id": source_id,
192:             "producer": producer,
193:             "observation_date": observed if observed else None,
194:             "attributed": attributed,
195:             "reasons": claim_reasons,
196:         })
197: 
198:     for kind in ("supporting", "opposing"):
199:         if sum(1 for item in items if item["kind"] == kind) > CLAIMS_PER_SIDE:
200:             note("CLAIM_LIMIT_EXCEEDED")
201: 
202:     attributed_details = [detail for detail in details if detail["attributed"]]
203:     if attributed_details:
204:         if not any(detail["source_id"] in _ANALYST_SOURCES for detail in attributed_details):
205:             note("NO_ANALYST_REFERENCE")
206:         if not any(detail["observation_date"] for detail in attributed_details):
207:             note("NO_DATED_OBSERVATION")
208: 
209:     return {
210:         "status": "DOWNGRADED" if reasons else "ACCEPTED",
211:         "reasons": reasons,
212:         "claims_total": len(items),
213:         "claims_attributed": len(attributed_details),
214:         "unsupported_claim_ids": unsupported,
215:         "claims": details,
216:     }
217: 
218: 
219: def render_claim_ledger(final_state: dict) -> str:
220:     """The markdown claim ledger: each factor with its source, date or UNSUPPORTED label."""
221:     items = _claim_items(final_state)
222:     if not items:
223:         return ""
224:     audit = audit_material_claims(final_state)
225:     details = {detail["id"]: detail for detail in audit["claims"]}
226:     status = (f"**Traceability: {audit['status']}** — "
227:               f"{audit['claims_attributed']} of {audit['claims_total']} material claims attributed.")
228:     if audit["reasons"]:
229:         status += " Reasons: " + ", ".join(audit["reasons"]) + "."
230: 
231:     lines = ["## Evidence Linkage", "", status]
232:     for label, kind in (("Supporting factors", "supporting"), ("Opposing factors", "opposing")):
233:         group = [item for item in items if item["kind"] == kind]
234:         if not group:
235:             continue
236:         lines.extend(["", f"### {label}"])
237:         for item in group:
238:             detail = details[item["id"]]
239:             line = f'- **{item["id"]}** "{item["text"]}" — '
240:             if detail["attributed"]:
241:                 line += f"source: `{item['source_id']}` ({detail['producer']})"
242:                 if detail["observation_date"]:
243:                     line += f" @ {detail['observation_date']}"
244:             else:
245:                 line += f"UNSUPPORTED ({', '.join(detail['reasons'])})"
246:             lines.append(line)
247:     return "\n".join(lines)
248: 
249: 
250: def _traceability_header(final_state: dict) -> str | None:
251:     """The header line for a report that carries material claims."""
252:     if not final_state.get("material_claims"):
253:         return None
254:     audit = audit_material_claims(final_state)
255:     line = (f"- Traceability: {audit['status']} "
256:             f"({audit['claims_attributed']}/{audit['claims_total']} material claims attributed)")
257:     if audit["reasons"]:
258:         line += f"; {', '.join(audit['reasons'])}"
259:     return line
260: 
261: 
262: # The report's sections in order: (heading, folder, [(agent, file, state path)]).
263: _SECTIONS = (
264:     ("I. Analyst Team Reports", "1_analysts", (
265:         ("Market Analyst", "market.md", ("market_report",)),
266:         ("Sentiment Analyst", "sentiment.md", ("sentiment_report",)),
267:         ("News Analyst", "news.md", ("news_report",)),
268:         ("Fundamentals Analyst", "fundamentals.md", ("fundamentals_report",)),
269:     )),
270:     ("II. Research Team Decision", "2_research", (
271:         ("Bull Researcher", "bull.md", ("investment_debate_state", "bull_history")),
272:         ("Bear Researcher", "bear.md", ("investment_debate_state", "bear_history")),
273:         ("Research Manager", "manager.md", ("investment_plan",)),
274:     )),
275:     ("III. Trading Team Plan", "3_trading", (
276:         ("Trader", "trader.md", ("trader_investment_plan",)),
277:     )),
278:     ("IV. Risk Management Team Decision", "4_risk", (
279:         ("Aggressive Analyst", "aggressive.md", ("risk_debate_state", "aggressive_history")),
280:         ("Conservative Analyst", "conservative.md", ("risk_debate_state", "conservative_history")),
281:         ("Neutral Analyst", "neutral.md", ("risk_debate_state", "neutral_history")),
282:     )),
283:     ("V. Portfolio Manager Decision", "5_portfolio", (
284:         ("Portfolio Manager", "decision.md", ("final_trade_decision",)),
285:     )),
286: )
287: 
288: 
289: def _report_parts(final_state: dict) -> list[tuple[str, str, list[tuple[str, str, str]]]]:
290:     """The sections with something to say: (heading, folder, [(agent, file, text)])."""
291:     parts = []
292:     for heading, folder, agents in _SECTIONS:
293:         written = []
294:         for agent, filename, path in agents:
295:             value = final_state
296:             for key in path:
297:                 value = (value or {}).get(key)
298:             if value:
299:                 written.append((agent, filename, value))
300:         if written:
301:             parts.append((heading, folder, written))
302:     return parts
303: 
304: 
305: def _report_document(ticker: str, final_state: dict, settings: dict | None,
306:                      sections: list[str]) -> str:
307:     """The complete markdown report: header, sections, claim ledger, disclaimer."""
308:     body = _header(ticker, final_state, settings) + "\n\n".join(sections)
309:     ledger = render_claim_ledger(final_state)
310:     if ledger:
311:         body += "\n\n" + ledger
312:     return body + "\n\n---\n\n" + NOT_INVESTMENT_ADVICE + "\n"
313: 
314: 
315: def write_report_tree(final_state: dict, ticker: str, save_path, settings: dict | None = None,
316:                       html: bool = True) -> Path:
317:     """Save a completed run's reports to ``save_path``; return the complete-report path.
318: 
319:     ``settings`` (``TradingAgentsGraph.run_settings()``) adds what produced the run
320:     to the report's header. ``html`` also writes ``complete_report.html``, one
321:     self-contained page of the same report (#1419).
322:     Reusing a directory replaces the generated report files, removing sections
323:     absent from this run and disabled HTML. Other files and directories are kept.
324:     """
325:     save_path = Path(save_path)
326:     save_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
327:     parts = _report_parts(final_state)
328:     sections = []
329:     for heading, folder, written in parts:
330:         (save_path / folder).mkdir(exist_ok=True)
331:         for _agent, filename, text in written:
332:             (save_path / folder / filename).write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
333:         content = "\n\n".join(f"### {agent}\n{text}" for agent, _filename, text in written)
334:         sections.append(f"## {heading}\n\n{content}")
335: 
336:     (save_path / "complete_report.md").write_text(
337:         _report_document(ticker, final_state, settings, sections), encoding="utf-8"
338:     )
339:     if html:
340:         from tradingagents.report_html import render_report
341: 
342:         (save_path / "complete_report.html").write_text(
343:             render_report(ticker, final_state, settings, parts), encoding="utf-8"
344:         )
345:     else:
346:         (save_path / "complete_report.html").unlink(missing_ok=True)
347: 
348:     # Reconcile only the filenames owned by this writer; never clear the tree,
349:     # since callers may keep notes or other artifacts alongside the reports.
350:     current_files = {
351:         (folder, filename)
352:         for _heading, folder, written in parts
353:         for _agent, filename, _text in written
354:     }
355:     for _heading, folder, agents in _SECTIONS:
356:         for _agent, filename, _path in agents:
357:             if (folder, filename) not in current_files:
358:                 (save_path / folder / filename).unlink(missing_ok=True)
359:     return save_path / "complete_report.md"

51: class TradingAgentsGraph:
52:     """Main class that orchestrates the trading agents framework."""

54:     def __init__(

56:         selected_analysts=("market", "social", "news", "fundamentals"),

62: 
63:         Args:
64:             selected_analysts: List of analyst types to include
65:             debug: Whether to run in debug mode
66:             config: Configuration dictionary. If None, uses default config
67:             callbacks: Optional list of callback handlers (e.g., for tracking LLM/tool stats)
68:         """

70:         self.config = config or DEFAULT_CONFIG
71:         self.callbacks = callbacks or []
72: 
73:         set_config(self.config)
74: 
75:         os.makedirs(self.config["data_cache_dir"], exist_ok=True)
76:         os.makedirs(self.config["results_dir"], exist_ok=True)
77: 
78:         extra = {"callbacks": self.callbacks} if self.callbacks else {}
79:         self.deep_thinking_llm = create_tier_client(self.config, "deep", **extra).get_llm()
80:         self.quick_thinking_llm = create_tier_client(self.config, "quick", **extra).get_llm()
81: 
82:         self.memory_log = TradingMemoryLog(self.config)
83: 
84:         self.conditional_logic = ConditionalLogic(
85:             max_debate_rounds=self.config["max_debate_rounds"],
86:             max_risk_discuss_rounds=self.config["max_risk_discuss_rounds"],
87:         )
88:         # An analyst takes two graph steps per tool round, plus its first turn
89:         # and its wrap-up; a limit past the recursion limit would end the run
90:         # there instead.
91:         max_tool_rounds, max_recur_limit = self.config["max_tool_rounds"], self.config["max_recur_limit"]
92:         if 2 * max_tool_rounds + 2 >= max_recur_limit:
93:             raise ValueError(
94:                 f"max_tool_rounds={max_tool_rounds} needs max_recur_limit above {2 * max_tool_rounds + 2}"
95:             )
96:         self.graph_setup = GraphSetup(
97:             self.quick_thinking_llm,
98:             self.deep_thinking_llm,
99:             self.conditional_logic,
100:             max_tool_rounds,
101:         )
102: 
103:         self.propagator = Propagator(
104:             max_recur_limit=max_recur_limit,
105:         )
106:         self.reflector = Reflector(self.quick_thinking_llm)
107: 
108:         # Graph-shape-affecting run choices, kept for the checkpoint signature.
109:         self.selected_analysts = tuple(selected_analysts)
110: 
111:         # Set up the graph: keep the workflow for recompilation with a checkpointer.
112:         self.workflow = self.graph_setup.setup_graph(selected_analysts, memory_node=self._memory_step)
113:         self.graph = self.workflow.compile()
114:         self._checkpointer_ctx = None
115:         self._resuming = False
