117:     def resolve_instrument_context(self, ticker: str, asset_type: str = "stock",
118:                                    trade_date: str | None = None) -> str:
119:         """Resolve ticker identity once and return the full instrument context.
120: 
121:         Deterministic yfinance lookup (cached, fail-open) injected into a
122:         context string so every agent anchors to the real company instead of
123:         hallucinating one from the price chart (#814). Both the propagate()
124:         path and the CLI call this so the resolved identity reaches the whole
125:         graph regardless of entry point.
126:         """
127:         identity = resolve_instrument_identity(ticker)
128:         return build_instrument_context(ticker, asset_type, identity, trade_date)
129: 
130:     def _memory_as_of(self, trade_date) -> str | None:
131:         """Point-in-time cutoff for past-context lessons (#1251).
132: 
133:         A historical/backtest run (trade date before today) filters lessons to
134:         those already resolved by the trade date. A current-date run returns
135:         None, disabling the filter so live behavior and pre-migration entries
136:         (which have no stored resolution date) are unaffected.
137:         """
138:         return str(trade_date) if is_historical(trade_date) else None
139: 
140:     def _run_signature(self, asset_type: str, portfolio=None) -> str:
141:         """Run inputs that must invalidate a checkpoint if changed.
142: 
143:         Keyed into the checkpoint thread ID so a resume under a different analyst
144:         selection, debate/risk depth, or asset mode starts fresh instead of
145:         silently continuing the previous graph (#1089). The rest of the config
146:         counts too (provider, models, endpoint, language, vendors, limits): a
147:         resume must not carry reports that other settings produced. Only where
148:         the run keeps its files and how it retries are left out.
149:         """
150:         settings = {k: v for k, v in self.config.items() if k not in _NOT_IN_SIGNATURE}
151:         digest = hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(settings, sort_keys=True, default=str).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]
152:         return "|".join([
153:             "analysts=" + ",".join(self.selected_analysts),
154:             f"debate={self.config['max_debate_rounds']}",
155:             f"risk={self.config['max_risk_discuss_rounds']}",
156:             f"asset={asset_type}",
157:             # None, an empty book and a changed book are three different runs.
158:             f"portfolio={portfolio.fingerprint() if portfolio is not None else 'none'}",
159:             # The layout itself: a checkpoint saved when analysts ran one after
160:             # another has pending nodes this graph no longer has, and one saved
161:             # before the Memory Log step would resume without the lessons.
162:             "analysts=parallel",
163:             "memory=parallel",
164:             f"settings={digest}",
165:         ])
166: 
167:     def propagate(self, company_name, trade_date, asset_type: str = "stock", portfolio=None):
168:         """Run the trading agents graph for a company on a specific date.
169: 
170:         ``asset_type`` selects between the stock pipeline (default) and the
171:         crypto pipeline (``"crypto"``) shipped in #567 — the CLI auto-detects
172:         from the ticker; programmatic callers pass it explicitly. When
173:         ``checkpoint_enabled`` is set in config, the graph is recompiled with
174:         a per-ticker SqliteSaver so a crashed run can resume from the last
175:         successful node on a subsequent invocation with the same ticker+date.
176: 
177:         Returns ``(final_state, signal)`` where ``signal`` is one of the 5-tier
178:         ratings (Buy / Overweight / Hold / Underweight / Sell) or ``"REVIEW"``
179:         when the decision had no parseable rating (#1170); guard with
180:         ``tradingagents.agents.rating.is_review`` before mapping it to the
181:         PortfolioRating enum.
182:         """
183:         trade_date = _validate_trade_date(trade_date)
184: 
185:         with run_config(self.config), \
186:                 self.checkpoint_scope(company_name, trade_date, asset_type, portfolio) as thread_id_value:
187:             return self._run_graph(
188:                 company_name, trade_date, asset_type=asset_type,
189:                 checkpoint_thread_id=thread_id_value, portfolio=portfolio,
190:             )
191: 
192:     def begin_checkpoint(self, company_name, trade_date, asset_type: str = "stock", portfolio=None) -> str | None:
193:         """Recompile the graph with a per-ticker checkpointer and return the
194:         ``thread_id`` to inject into the stream/invoke ``config`` (or ``None``
195:         when checkpointing is disabled).
196: 
197:         Pair every call with :meth:`end_checkpoint` in a ``finally``. Both
198:         ``propagate`` (via :meth:`checkpoint_scope`) and the CLI stream path use
199:         this so ``--checkpoint`` actually resumes (#1249); previously the setup
200:         lived only inside ``propagate`` and the CLI streamed the checkpointer-less
201:         graph, making the flag a no-op.
202:         """
203:         self._resuming = False
204:         if not self.config.get("checkpoint_enabled"):
205:             return None
206:         signature = self._run_signature(asset_type, portfolio)
207:         self._checkpointer_ctx = get_checkpointer(self.config["data_cache_dir"], company_name)
208:         saver = self._checkpointer_ctx.__enter__()
209:         self.graph = self.workflow.compile(checkpointer=saver)
210: 
211:         step = checkpoint_step(
212:             self.config["data_cache_dir"], company_name, str(trade_date), signature
213:         )
214:         self._resuming = step is not None
215:         if step is not None:
216:             logger.info("Resuming from step %d for %s on %s", step, company_name, trade_date)
217:         else:
218:             logger.info("Starting fresh for %s on %s", company_name, trade_date)
219:         return thread_id(company_name, str(trade_date), signature)
220: 
221:     def checkpoint_input(self, init_state):
222:         """The value to stream/invoke: ``None`` to resume an existing checkpoint,
223:         else the initial state for a fresh run.
224: 
225:         LangGraph resumes an interrupted thread when invoked with ``None``;
226:         re-passing the initial state instead appends it through the message
227:         reducer, duplicating messages in the resumed state (#1249).
228:         """
229:         return None if self._resuming else init_state
230: 
231:     def end_checkpoint(self):
232:         """Restore the plain uncheckpointed graph after a checkpointed run."""
233:         if self._checkpointer_ctx is not None:
234:             self._checkpointer_ctx.__exit__(None, None, None)
235:             self._checkpointer_ctx = None
236:             self.graph = self.workflow.compile()
237:         self._resuming = False
238: 
239:     @contextmanager
240:     def checkpoint_scope(self, company_name, trade_date, asset_type: str = "stock", portfolio=None):
241:         """Context-manager form of begin/end_checkpoint for the propagate path."""
242:         try:
243:             yield self.begin_checkpoint(company_name, trade_date, asset_type, portfolio)
244:         finally:
245:             self.end_checkpoint()
246: 
247:     def clear_checkpoint_on_success(self, company_name, trade_date, asset_type: str = "stock", portfolio=None):
248:         """Drop a completed run's checkpoint so a later run starts fresh (#1249)."""
249:         if self.config.get("checkpoint_enabled"):
250:             clear_checkpoint(
251:                 self.config["data_cache_dir"], company_name, str(trade_date),
252:                 self._run_signature(asset_type, portfolio),
253:             )
254: 
255:     def run_settings(self) -> dict:
256:         """What produces this graph's runs, for the saved report and state log.
257: 
258:         An allowlist: endpoints (a backend_url can carry credentials), keys and
259:         local paths are never recorded.
260:         """
261:         cfg = self.config
262:         return {
263:             "version": tradingagents.__version__,
264:             "llm_provider": cfg.get("llm_provider"),
265:             "deep_think_provider": tier_provider(cfg, "deep") if cfg.get("llm_provider") else None,
266:             "deep_think_llm": cfg.get("deep_think_llm"),
267:             "quick_think_provider": tier_provider(cfg, "quick") if cfg.get("llm_provider") else None,
268:             "quick_think_llm": cfg.get("quick_think_llm"),
269:             "analysts": list(self.selected_analysts),
270:             "max_debate_rounds": cfg.get("max_debate_rounds"),
271:             "max_risk_discuss_rounds": cfg.get("max_risk_discuss_rounds"),
272:             "output_language": cfg.get("output_language"),
273:             "data_vendors": dict(cfg.get("data_vendors") or {}),
274:             "tool_vendors": dict(cfg.get("tool_vendors") or {}),
275:         }
276: 
277:     def save_reports(self, final_state, ticker, save_path=None, html=True) -> Path:
278:         """Write the report tree for a completed run, like the CLI does.
279: 
280:         Programmatic callers get the same on-disk reports the CLI produces. Pass
281:         an explicit ``save_path`` or let it default under ``results_dir``; the
282:         report is also written as one HTML page unless ``html`` is False.
283:         """
284:         if save_path is None:
285:             save_path = self.default_report_path(ticker)
286:         return write_report_tree(final_state, ticker, save_path, settings=self.run_settings(), html=html)
287: 
288:     def default_report_path(self, ticker) -> Path:
289:         """Where a run's reports go unless told otherwise: under results_dir, stamped now."""
290:         stamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
291:         return Path(self.config["results_dir"]) / "reports" / f"{safe_ticker_component(ticker)}_{stamp}"
292: 
293:     def create_run_state(self, company_name, trade_date, asset_type: str = "stock", portfolio=None):
294:         """Build a run's initial state; propagate() and the CLI both start here.
295: 
296:         Injects the resolved instrument identity for every agent (#814). The
297:         memory log's lessons are not here: the graph's Memory Log step settles
298:         and loads them alongside the analysts (see ``_memory_step``).
299:         """
300:         trade_date = _validate_trade_date(trade_date)
301:         return self.propagator.create_initial_state(
302:             company_name,
303:             trade_date,
304:             asset_type=asset_type,
305:             instrument_context=self.resolve_instrument_context(company_name, asset_type, trade_date),
306:             portfolio_context=portfolio.render(company_name) if portfolio is not None else "",
307:         )
308: 
309:     def _memory_step(self, state):
310:         """The graph's Memory Log step, alongside the analysts (#1428): settle every
311:         ticker's due decisions (#1445), then return the lessons known by the
312:         trade date for the Portfolio Manager (#1251).
313: 
314:         Settling fetches prices and asks the model for a reflection per decision,
315:         so it runs beside the analysts instead of before them. A failure here
316:         does not end the run: the analysts' work is kept, the lessons already in
317:         the log are used, and the report says what could not be settled.
318:         """
319:         note = ""
320:         try:
321:             # Another run settling the same log does the work; this one goes on.
322:             done = self.settle_all_pending(wait=False)
323:             if done.failed:
324:                 note = (f"{len(done.failed)} past decision(s) could not be settled this run "
325:                         "and stay pending.")
326:         except Exception as exc:
327:             logger.warning("Settling past decisions failed: %s", exc)
328:             note = f"Past decisions could not be settled this run ({type(exc).__name__}); they stay pending."
329:         try:
330:             past_context = self.memory_log.get_past_context(
331:                 state["company_of_interest"], as_of=self._memory_as_of(state["trade_date"]))
332:         except Exception as exc:
333:             logger.warning("Reading the memory log failed: %s", exc)
334:             past_context = ""
335:             note = note or f"The memory log could not be read this run ({type(exc).__name__})."
336:         return {"past_context": past_context, "memory_note": note}
337: 
338:     def settle_pending(self, company_name) -> settlement.Settlement:
339:         """Settle this ticker's decisions whose holding window has now traded.
340: 
341:         A run settles every due decision alongside its analysts, so its own
342:         decision stays pending until a later run. A caller that is done
343:         analyzing a ticker (a backtest sweep, a scheduled job) calls this to
344:         settle it now. Returns what was settled and what failed.
345:         """
346:         with run_config(self.config):
347:             return settlement.settle_pending(company_name, self.memory_log, self.reflector, self.config)
348: 
349:     def settle_all_pending(self, wait: bool = True) -> settlement.Settlement:
350:         """Settle every ticker's decisions whose holding window has now traded (#1445).
351: 
352:         For a scheduler whose tickers rotate: a ticker it stops analysing would
353:         otherwise keep its decisions pending, and their lessons out of later runs.
354:         With ``wait=False`` a pass already running on the same log is not waited for.
355:         """
356:         with run_config(self.config):
357:             return settlement.settle_all_pending(self.memory_log, self.reflector, self.config, wait=wait)
358: 
359:     def record_decision(self, company_name, trade_date, final_state):
360:         """Record a finished run: its state log, and its decision in the memory log
361:         for reflection on the next same-ticker run. propagate() and the CLI both end here."""
362:         audit = audit_material_claims(final_state)
363:         final_state["claim_audit"] = audit
364:         self._log_state(trade_date, final_state)
365:         decision = final_state.get("final_trade_decision")
366:         if not decision:
367:             logger.warning("No final decision for %s on %s; nothing added to the memory log",
368:                            company_name, trade_date)
369:             return
370:         self.memory_log.store_decision(
371:             ticker=company_name, trade_date=trade_date, final_trade_decision=decision,
372:             rating=run_rating(final_state),
373:         )
374: 
375:     def _run_graph(self, company_name, trade_date, asset_type: str = "stock",
376:                    checkpoint_thread_id: str | None = None, portfolio=None):
377:         """Execute the graph and write the resulting state to disk and memory log."""
378:         init_agent_state = self.create_run_state(company_name, trade_date, asset_type, portfolio)
379:         args = self.propagator.get_graph_args()
380: 
381:         # Inject the checkpoint thread_id (from checkpoint_scope) so the same
382:         # ticker+date+graph-shape resumes; a different one starts fresh (#1089).
383:         if checkpoint_thread_id is not None:
384:             args.setdefault("config", {}).setdefault("configurable", {})["thread_id"] = checkpoint_thread_id
385: 
386:         # None resumes an existing checkpoint; init_agent_state starts fresh (#1249).
387:         graph_input = self.checkpoint_input(init_agent_state)
388:         if self.debug:
389:             # A state repeats the messages before it, so each prints once (#1027).
390:             final_state, printed = {}, set()
391:             for messages, state in self.stream_run(graph_input, **args):
392:                 for msg in messages:
393:                     key = getattr(msg, "id", None) or (type(msg).__name__, getattr(msg, "content", None))
394:                     if key not in printed:
395:                         printed.add(key)
396:                         msg.pretty_print()
397:                 if state is not None:
398:                     final_state.update(state)
399:         else:
400:             final_state = self.graph.invoke(graph_input, **args)
401: 
402:         self.record_decision(company_name, trade_date, final_state)
403: 
404:         # Clear checkpoint on successful completion to avoid stale state.
405:         self.clear_checkpoint_on_success(company_name, trade_date, asset_type, portfolio)
406: 
407:         return final_state, run_rating(final_state)
408: 
409:     def stream_run(self, graph_input, **args):
410:         """Stream a run as ``(messages, state)`` pairs.
411: 
412:         ``messages`` are the agents' messages, the analysts' included. ``state``
413:         is the run's state after a top-level step; for a step inside an analyst's
414:         graph it is that analyst's report once filed, else None.
415: 
416:         Each analyst works in a graph of its own, and the run's state takes the
417:         analysts' reports only when the slowest has finished, so their messages
418:         and reports come from their own finished steps ("tasks") as they happen.
419:         """
420:         args = {**args, "stream_mode": ["values", "tasks"]}
421:         # The graph's own config serves every tool call, as in propagate(), even
422:         # when the process-wide config has changed since. Each step runs in the
423:         # run's context, so the caller keeps its own between steps.
424:         context = run_config_context(self.config)
425:         stream = context.run(self.graph.stream, graph_input, subgraphs=True, **args)
426:         try:
427:             while (step := context.run(next, stream, None)) is not None:
428:                 namespace, mode, chunk = step
429:                 if namespace:
430:                     result = chunk.get("result") if mode == "tasks" else None
431:                     if isinstance(result, dict):
432:                         report = {k: v for k, v in result.items() if k != "messages" and v}
433:                         if result.get("messages") or report:
434:                             yield result.get("messages", []), report or None
435:                 elif mode == "values":
436:                     yield chunk.get("messages", []), chunk
437:         finally:
438:             context.run(stream.close)
439: 
440:     def _log_state(self, trade_date, final_state):
441:         """Write a run's final state to JSON under the run's own ticker."""
442:         entry = {
443:             "company_of_interest": final_state["company_of_interest"],
444:             "trade_date": final_state["trade_date"],
445:             "market_report": final_state["market_report"],
446:             "sentiment_report": final_state["sentiment_report"],
447:             "news_report": final_state["news_report"],
448:             "fundamentals_report": final_state["fundamentals_report"],
449:             "investment_debate_state": {
450:                 "bull_history": final_state["investment_debate_state"]["bull_history"],
451:                 "bear_history": final_state["investment_debate_state"]["bear_history"],
452:                 "history": final_state["investment_debate_state"]["history"],
453:                 "current_response": final_state["investment_debate_state"][
454:                     "current_response"
455:                 ],
456:             },
457:             "trader_investment_plan": final_state["trader_investment_plan"],
458:             "risk_debate_state": {
459:                 "aggressive_history": final_state["risk_debate_state"]["aggressive_history"],
460:                 "conservative_history": final_state["risk_debate_state"]["conservative_history"],
461:                 "neutral_history": final_state["risk_debate_state"]["neutral_history"],
462:                 "history": final_state["risk_debate_state"]["history"],
463:             },
464:             "investment_plan": final_state["investment_plan"],
465:             "final_trade_decision": final_state["final_trade_decision"],
466:             "final_rating": run_rating(final_state),
467:             "claim_audit": final_state.get("claim_audit"),
468:             "material_claims": final_state.get("material_claims", []),
469:             "claim_fallback": final_state.get("claim_fallback"),
470:             "run_settings": self.run_settings(),
471:         }
472: 
473:         # A ticker that would escape the results directory is rejected.
474:         safe_ticker = safe_ticker_component(final_state["company_of_interest"])
475:         directory = Path(self.config["results_dir"]) / safe_ticker / "TradingAgentsStrategy_logs"
476:         directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
477: 
478:         log_path = directory / f"full_states_log_{trade_date}.json"
479:         with open(log_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
480:             # Reports can be in any language and this file is read by a person.
481:             json.dump(entry, f, indent=4, ensure_ascii=False)

5: ACCEPTED (ADR-001 §4).
6: """

32:     return state

1: """claim_audit is recorded with the run; claim_enforcement stays inert (ADR-001).
2: 
3: record_decision computes the pure audit before writing the state log, so every
4: run's log carries its ACCEPTED / DOWNGRADED / NOT_ASSESSED status. The reserved
5: claim_enforcement flag is configurable but no behaviour branches on it.
6: """
7: 
8: import json
9: 
10: import pytest
11:

13: from tradingagents.graph.trading_graph import TradingAgentsGraph
14: from tradingagents.memory import TradingMemoryLog
15: 
16: TRADE_DATE = "2026-11-14"
17: 
18: 
19: def _graph(tmp_path):
20:     graph = object.__new__(TradingAgentsGraph)
21:     graph.config = {
22:         "results_dir": str(tmp_path), "llm_provider": "openai",
23:         "deep_think_llm": "d", "quick_think_llm": "q",
24:         "max_debate_rounds": 1, "max_risk_discuss_rounds": 1,
25:         "output_language": "English", "data_vendors": {}, "tool_vendors": {},
26:         "claim_enforcement": False,
27:     }
28:     graph.selected_analysts = ("market",)
29:     graph.memory_log = TradingMemoryLog({"memory_log_path": str(tmp_path / "m.md")})
30:     return graph
31: 
32: 
33: def _state(**overrides):
34:     state = {
35:         "company_of_interest": "NVDA",
36:         "trade_date": TRADE_DATE,
37:         "market_report": "Market report.",
38:         "sentiment_report": "",
39:         "news_report": "",
40:         "fundamentals_report": "",
41:         "investment_debate_state": {"bull_history": "", "bear_history": "",
42:                                     "history": "", "current_response": ""},
43:         "trader_investment_plan": "Trader plan.",
44:         "investment_plan": "Plan.",
45:         "risk_debate_state": {"aggressive_history": "", "conservative_history": "",
46:                               "neutral_history": "", "history": ""},
47:         "final_trade_decision": "**Rating**: Hold",
48:         "final_rating": "Hold",
49:     }
50:     state.update(overrides)
51:     return state
52: 
53: 
54: def _logged(tmp_path):
55:     path = next(tmp_path.rglob(f"full_states_log_{TRADE_DATE}.json"))
56:     return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
57: 
58:

1: """The whole graph, end to end, with scripted models and no network.
2: 
3: Every tool-using analyst calls each of its tools once through the vendor router,
4: the debates and managers run, and the decision is parsed and logged. This pins
5: the wiring: a restructure that drops a node, a tool or an edge fails here.
6: """
7: 
8: from __future__ import annotations
9: 
10: import copy
11: import time
12: 
13: import pandas as pd
14: import pytest
15: from langchain_core.language_models.chat_models import BaseChatModel
16: from langchain_core.messages import AIMessage, HumanMessage, ToolMessage
17: from langchain_core.outputs import ChatGeneration, ChatResult
18: from langchain_core.runnables import RunnableLambda
19: from pydantic import Field
20: 
21: from tradingagents.agents import context, schemas
22: from tradingagents.agents.analysts import sentiment_analyst
23: from tradingagents.dataflows import router
24: from tradingagents.dataflows.vendors.yahoo import market as yahoo_market, snapshot
25: from tradingagents.default_config import DEFAULT_CONFIG
26: from tradingagents.graph import trading_graph
27: 
28: TRADE_DATE = "2026-01-09"
29: 
30: # Enough for every free-text reader: the PM's labelled rating and the trader's
31: # closing proposal line.
32: TEXT = "Report.\n\n**Rating**: Overweight\n\nFINAL TRANSACTION PROPOSAL: **BUY**"
33: 
34: STRUCTURED = {
35:     schemas.ResearchPlan: schemas.ResearchPlan(
36:         recommendation=schemas.PortfolioRating.OVERWEIGHT, rationale="r", strategic_actions="a"),
37:     schemas.TraderProposal: schemas.TraderProposal(action=schemas.TraderAction.BUY, reasoning="r"),
38:     # The thesis quotes another party's rating; the decision is still the PM's own.
39:     schemas.PortfolioDecision: schemas.PortfolioDecision(
40:         rating=schemas.PortfolioRating.OVERWEIGHT, confidence="medium",
41:         executive_summary="s",
42:         investment_thesis="Street consensus rating: Buy (28 of 35 analysts)."),
43:     schemas.SentimentReport: schemas.SentimentReport(
44:         overall_band=schemas.SentimentBand.NEUTRAL, overall_score=5.0, confidence="low", narrative="n"),
45: }
46: 
47: ARGS = {"symbol": "NVDA", "ticker": "NVDA", "curr_date": TRADE_DATE, "start_date": "2026-01-02",
48:         "end_date": TRADE_DATE, "indicator": "rsi", "topic": "Fed rate cut", "freq": "quarterly"}
49: 
50: 
51: class ScriptedModel(BaseChatModel):
52:     """Calls every bound tool once, then answers with TEXT."""
53: 
54:     structured: bool = False
55:     tools: tuple = ()
56:     calls: list = Field(default_factory=list)   # shared across bound copies
57:     threads: set = Field(default_factory=set)   # threads that served a tool-bound call
58:     fail_at: int | None = None                  # raise on this call, once
59: 
60:     @property
61:     def _llm_type(self) -> str:
62:         return "scripted"
63: 
64:     def bind_tools(self, tools, **kwargs):
65:         return self.model_copy(update={"tools": tuple(tools)})
66: 
67:     def with_structured_output(self, schema, **kwargs):
68:         if not self.structured:
69:             raise NotImplementedError
70:         return RunnableLambda(lambda _: self._count() or STRUCTURED[schema])
71: 
72:     def _count(self) -> None:
73:         self.calls.append(1)
74:         if len(self.calls) == self.fail_at:
75:             raise RuntimeError("provider unavailable")
76: 
77:     def _generate(self, messages, stop=None, run_manager=None, **kwargs) -> ChatResult:
78:         self._count()
79:         if self.tools:
80:             import threading
81:             import time
82:             self.threads.add(threading.current_thread().name)
83:             time.sleep(0.05)   # long enough for concurrent analysts to overlap
84:         if self.tools and not isinstance(messages[-1], ToolMessage):
85:             calls = [{"name": t.name, "id": f"call_{i}",
86:                       "args": {k: v for k, v in ARGS.items()
87:                                if k in t.tool_call_schema.model_json_schema()["properties"]}}
88:                      for i, t in enumerate(self.tools)]
89:             message = AIMessage(content="", tool_calls=calls)
90:         else:
91:             message = AIMessage(content=TEXT)
92:         return ChatResult(generations=[ChatGeneration(message=message)])
93: 
94: 
95: class _Client:
96:     def __init__(self, model):
97:         self.model = model
98: 
99:     def get_llm(self):
100:         return self.model
101: 
102:

119:     monkeypatch.setattr(yahoo_market.yf, "Ticker", lambda s: type("T", (), {"info": {"longName": "NVIDIA"}})())
120:     context._identity.cache_clear()

239: class LoopingModel(ScriptedModel):
240:     """Calls a tool on every turn it is offered one (#1420)."""
241: 
242:     tool_turns: list = Field(default_factory=list)    # shared across bound copies
243:     last_turns: list = Field(default_factory=list)    # histories of the turns offered no tools
244: 
245:     def _generate(self, messages, stop=None, run_manager=None, **kwargs) -> ChatResult:
246:         if not self.tools:
247:             self.last_turns.append(messages)
248:             return super()._generate(messages, stop, run_manager, **kwargs)
249:         self.tool_turns.append(1)
250:         tool = self.tools[0]
251:         call = {"name": tool.name, "id": f"call_{len(self.tool_turns)}",
252:                 "args": {k: v for k, v in ARGS.items()
253:                          if k in tool.tool_call_schema.model_json_schema()["properties"]}}
254:         return ChatResult(generations=[ChatGeneration(message=AIMessage(content="", tool_calls=[call]))])
255: 
256:

262:

266:     for key in ("market_report", "news_report", "fundamentals_report"):
267:         assert final_state[key] == TEXT
268:     assert rating == "Overweight"

294: class TimedModel(ScriptedModel):
295:     """ScriptedModel that records each call's graph step, prompt and timing."""
296: 
297:     seen: list = Field(default_factory=list)    # shared across bound copies
298: 
299:     def _generate(self, messages, stop=None, run_manager=None, **kwargs) -> ChatResult:
300:         step = (run_manager.metadata or {}).get("langgraph_node") if run_manager else None
301:         started = time.monotonic()
302:         if step in {"Market Analyst", "agent"} or step == "Memory Log":
303:             time.sleep(0.2)                     # long enough to see what overlaps
304:         result = super()._generate(messages, stop, run_manager, **kwargs)
305:         prompt = "\n".join(str(m.content) for m in messages)
306:         self.seen.append((step, prompt, started, time.monotonic()))
307:         return result
308:

329:     assert lessons

331:     assert lessons in pm_prompt

Now the plan itself. Let me construct carefully.

### Task restatement

Activating fail-closed claim traceability, deferred in ADR-001 §4. Scope = REQ-TA-FR-005 (accepted-report contract), QR-002 (unconditional attribution), INV-005 (no untraceable claim in an accepted recommendation). Constraint = INV-001 (REVIEW rule unchanged), FR-004 boundary (validation gate not touched), FR-006 (candidate/wont → run-status enum NOT available → out of scope).

What "accepted" means today: `audit_material_claims` returns ACCEPTED/DOWNGRADED/NOT_ASSESSED and it is only recorded, never used. No code path reads claim_audit except the state log. So a report whose claims are untraceable is still presented with full ranking/rating.

Deliverable: a run-level acceptance verdict derived from the audit, computed once at record_decision, stored on state, and honoured at the two presentation surfaces (report markdown/HTML header + evidence section, and state log), gated by claim_enforcement; `final_rating` untouched.

### Traceability table

Requirement ↔ step ↔ file.

Steps:
S1 contract/state fields
S2 enforcement function in reporting.py (pure): `claim_acceptance(audit, enforced)` maybe — or `evaluate_claim_acceptance(final_state, enforced)`.
S3 wire into record_decision (compute once, store) + state log key
S4 render: markdown header/ledger + notice; HTML
S5 config default + docstring of claim_enforcement
S6 tests
S7 docs: ADR-001 amendment (§4 → activated), trace-matrix rows, ai-use-log

Requirement mapping:
- FR-005 (fit: "in a sample report every principal reason carries a source identifier..." + disclaimer): S4/S6 (rendering, disclaimer unchanged, notice added)
- QR-002 (fit: "any untraceable claim makes the report not accepted"): S2 (verdict), S3 (recorded), S6
- INV-005 (zero-tolerance, negative test): S2, S3, S6 negative tests
- INV-001 (constraint, must not break): S3 + regression test

Design of the pure function:

```python
def claim_acceptance(final_state: dict, *, enforced: bool) -> dict:
    """ADR-001 §4 activation: the run-level acceptance verdict from the claim audit."""
```
returns
```python
{
  "status": "ACCEPTED" | "REJECTED" | "NOT_ENFORCED",
  "audit_status": "ACCEPTED" | "DOWNGRADED" | "NOT_ASSESSED",
  "enforced": bool,
  "reasons": list[str],     # from the audit, plus "CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT_DISABLED" when not enforced
  "notice": str | None,     # CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE when status == REJECTED else None
}
```

Vocabulary closed: {ACCEPTED, REJECTED, NOT_ENFORCED}. Never a rating. Never FR-006 status.

Rule table:
| enforced | audit.status | claim_acceptance.status | accepted? |
| True | ACCEPTED | ACCEPTED | yes |
| True | DOWNGRADED | REJECTED | no |
| True | NOT_ASSESSED | REJECTED | no |
| False | any | NOT_ENFORCED | not claimed |

Note: NOT_ASSESSED → REJECTED under enforcement (matches ADR §3 "NOT_ASSESSED / NO_CLAIM_METADATA is not acceptance").

# 1. Task restatement

**What I am activating:** the fail-closed step that ADR-001 §4 explicitly deferred — *"Run acceptance is still not gated on the audit… Activating fail-closed — gating run acceptance on the audit and mapping the status onto the REQ-TA-FR-006 run-status enum — is a later change."* This task authorises the first half of that sentence only; the second half is out of scope (see "Boundaries").

| REQ ID | Status today | What changes |
|---|---|---|
| **REQ-TA-FR-005** | `implement` | The report must not present a recommendation whose principal reasons are untraceable as an evidence-linked recommendation. Ledger/disclaimer behaviour is preserved; a non-acceptance notice is added at both rendering surfaces. |
| **REQ-TA-QR-002** | `implement` | Fit criterion says *"any untraceable claim makes the report not accepted"*. Today nothing enforces that: `audit_material_claims` is computed in `record_decision` and only written to the state log. A run-level acceptance verdict is derived from it and made authoritative. |
| **REQ-TA-INV-005** | `implement` | The zero-tolerance invariant gains its run-path negative test: an untraceable claim now prevents acceptance rather than merely being labelled. UNSUPPORTED labelling and visibility are kept (nothing silently dropped). |
| **REQ-TA-INV-001** | `restate` | **Constraint, not scope.** The verdict must never touch `final_rating`; REVIEW stays REVIEW. Negative test required. |
| **REQ-TA-FR-004** | `implement` | **Constraint, not scope.** The validation gate and rating parser are untouched; a REVIEW decision plus a REJECTED verdict must both hold at once. |

**Boundaries (where I stop):**

1. **REQ-TA-FR-006 is `candidate` / `priority: wont`.** Its `COMPLETED / COMPLETED_WITH_WARNINGS / INSUFFICIENT_DATA / AGENT_DISAGREEMENT_REQUIRES_REVIEW` enum is *not* available to me (Prompt.md Step 1: stop at `candidate`, never invent). So the verdict I add is a **claim-traceability acceptance status**, closed to its own three values — it is explicitly *not* a run-status enum, and I will not add FR-006 fields, statuses or reason codes.
2. **No registry change.** FR-005, QR-002, INV-005 are already `implement`; nothing in `docs/req/requirements.yaml` needs editing. If the human wants FR-006 mapping, that is a separate re-plan with a registry change + ADR.
3. **No threshold invented.** The verdict is boolean per ADR-001 §5 (unconditional, no fraction), so no numeric threshold arises and no `TBD` is resolved by guesswork.
4. **No prompt change.** Claims are already produced inside the existing PM structured call; `prompts/**` is untouched, so the semantic-evaluation gate is not triggered.
5. **No new cross-module API:** `propagate()` keeps returning `(final_state, signal)`; the verdict rides on `AgentState`. This is the AGENTS.md §6 proposal below.

## 2.2 Interface contract (proposed — AGENTS.md §6 requires your approval)

### New `AgentState` field — exactly one

```python
claim_acceptance: Annotated[
    dict,
    "The claim-traceability acceptance verdict for this run (ADR-001 §4; "
    "REQ-TA-QR-002, REQ-TA-INV-005): status, audit_status, enforced, reasons, notice",
]
```

I deliberately add **one** dict, not a verdict + a flat status string: a second field would duplicate `claim_audit['status']` and can diverge.

### Verdict object — exact keys

```python
{
  "status":       "ACCEPTED" | "REJECTED" | "NOT_ENFORCED",   # closed vocabulary
  "audit_status": "ACCEPTED" | "DOWNGRADED" | "NOT_ASSESSED", # verbatim claim_audit["status"]
  "enforced":     bool,          # value of config["claim_enforcement"] used for this verdict
  "reasons":      list[str],     # audit reasons, copied; plus "CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT_DISABLED"
  "notice":       str | None,    # CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE when status == "REJECTED", else None
}
```

### Status vocabulary (closed; three values, no others)

| `claim_enforcement` | `claim_audit.status` | `claim_acceptance.status` | Report accepted? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `True` | `ACCEPTED` | `ACCEPTED` | yes |
| `True` | `DOWNGRADED` | `REJECTED` | no |
| `True` | `NOT_ASSESSED` | `REJECTED` | no — ADR §3: "NOT_ASSESSED … is **not** acceptance" |
| `False` | any | `NOT_ENFORCED` | **not claimed** — never `ACCEPTED` |

Rules that keep this inside its lane:

- It is **not** a rating. `final_rating`, `parse_rating`, `RATING_REVIEW`, `is_review` are untouched (INV-001).
- It is **not** a run status. No `COMPLETED*` value can be produced by this code (FR-006 boundary).
- `NOT_ENFORCED` exists so that the unenforced path cannot accidentally read as acceptance — the same honesty device as `NOT_ASSESSED`.
- Reason codes: the eight existing audit codes are reused verbatim; the only new keyword is `CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT_DISABLED`.

### Function and constant contract in `reporting.py`

```python
CLAIM_ACCEPTANCE_STATUSES = frozenset({"ACCEPTED", "REJECTED", "NOT_ENFORCED"})
CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE = (...)

def evaluate_claim_acceptance(final_state: dict, *, enforced: bool) -> dict:
    """Pure. Derives the ADR-001 §4 acceptance verdict from audit_material_claims."""
```

Pure, no I/O, no LLM — testable offline, and callable from renderers when a state was never put through `record_decision` (defensive path for `write_report_tree` on a raw state; default `enforced=None` ⇒ read `final_state.get("claim_acceptance")`, else compute with `enforced=True`).

### Run-path wiring contract

| Caller | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `TradingAgentsGraph.record_decision` | computes the verdict once (`enforced=self.config.get("claim_enforcement", False)`), assigns `final_state["claim_acceptance"]` **before** `_log_state`, exactly as `claim_audit` is done today |
| `_log_state` | one new key `"claim_acceptance"` next to `"claim_audit"` |
| `write_report_tree` / `_header` / `_report_document` | reads `final_state["claim_acceptance"]`; emits `- Acceptance: REJECTED; reasons: …` **only when not accepted**, and the notice paragraph before `## Evidence Linkage` |
| `report_html.render_report` | one `Acceptance` detail row when not accepted + the notice in the evidence section |
| `propagate()` / `stream_run()` / CLI | **unchanged signature and return value**; consumers read the state key |

`claim_enforcement` stays **out of `run_settings()`** on purpose: `run_settings()` feeds `_run_signature()`, so adding a key would invalidate every existing checkpoint. The verdict records `enforced` itself, which satisfies observability without that churn.

## 2.3 Files to change vs. files untouched

**Change**

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `src/tradingagents/agents/state.py` | +1 field (`claim_acceptance`) |
| `src/tradingagents/reporting.py` | `evaluate_claim_acceptance`, `CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE`, acceptance header line, notice in document |
| `src/tradingagents/report_html.py` | acceptance row + notice |
| `src/tradingagents/graph/trading_graph.py` | `record_decision` computes/stores verdict; `_log_state` adds the key |
| `src/tradingagents/default_config.py` | default value + comment (depends on decision **D1**) |
| `test/offline/test_claim_acceptance.py` | new file |
| `test/offline/test_claim_audit_recording.py` | 2 existing tests updated to the new contract (**test-approval gate**) |
| `docs/architecture/adr/001-material-claims.md` | §4 amended (deferral lifted, narrowed), §7 condition 3 marked done |
| `docs/architecture/trace-matrix.md` | INV-005/QR-002 rows: run-path wiring no longer "deferred"; add `claim_acceptance` monitor |

New file `test/offline/test_claim_acceptance.py` (mirrors the existing style: module docstring citing IDs, `@pytest.mark.unit`, `_graph()`/`_state()` helpers as in `test_claim_audit_recording.py`).

| `test_the_verdict_never_changes_the_final_rating` (parametrised over ACCEPTED / DOWNGRADED / NOT_ASSESSED) | **INV-001** zero-tolerance negative test: rating unchanged in every case, no exception |
| `test_a_review_rating_stays_review_whatever_the_verdict` | **INV-001** + FR-004 coexistence: REVIEW survives `ACCEPTED` and `REJECTED` |

```python
CLAIM_ACCEPTANCE_STATUSES = frozenset({"ACCEPTED", "REJECTED", "NOT_ENFORCED"})

CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE = (
    "This report is not accepted as an evidence-linked recommendation: ..."
)


def evaluate_claim_acceptance(final_state: dict, *, enforced: bool) -> dict:
    audit = audit_material_claims(final_state)
    reasons = list(audit["reasons"])
    if not enforced:
        status = "NOT_ENFORCED"
        if "CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT_DISABLED" not in reasons:
            reasons.append("CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT_DISABLED")
        notice = None
    elif audit["status"] == "ACCEPTED":
        status, notice = "ACCEPTED", None
    else:
        status, notice = "REJECTED", CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE
    return {
        "status": status,
        "audit_status": audit["status"],
        "enforced": enforced,
        "reasons": reasons,
        "notice": notice,
    }
```

```python
def claim_acceptance_of(final_state: dict) -> dict:
    """The verdict recorded for this run, or the one this state would get.

    A state that never went through ``record_decision`` (a hand-built state, a
    report written from a partial run) is judged as if enforcement were on, so a
    report never renders as accepted by accident.
    """
    recorded = final_state.get("claim_acceptance")
    if isinstance(recorded, dict) and recorded.get("status") in CLAIM_ACCEPTANCE_STATUSES:
        return recorded
    return evaluate_claim_acceptance(final_state, enforced=True)
```

Implements REQ-TA-QR-002 and REQ-TA-INV-005: an\n    untraceable material claim — or no claim metadata at all — prevents\n    acceptance. With enforcement off the verdict says so instead of claiming\n    acceptance.             f'{escape(notice)}{reasons}</p>')

```python
        return None
    return verdict.get("notice") or CLAIM_NOT_CERTIFIED_NOTICE
```

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"ADR-001 fail-closed: a run whose material claims do not resolve is not accepted.\n\nThese tests pin the acceptance verdict that REQ-TA-QR-002 and REQ-TA-INV-005 ask\nfor: every formally defined material claim resolves to a report artifact and a\nderivable producing agent, and an untraceable claim — or no claim metadata at\nall — prevents acceptance. The verdict is a report property, so REQ-TA-INV-001\nholds throughout: it never changes the rating, and REVIEW stays REVIEW.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE,\n    audit_material_claims,\n    evaluate_claim_acceptance,\n    write_report_tree,\n)\n\nTRADE_DATE = \"2026-11-14\"\n\n# The REQ-TA-FR-006 run-status enum is a candidate requirement, not this slice's\n# to introduce: no verdict may ever carry one of its values.\n_FR_006_STATUSES = frozenset({\n    \"COMPLETED\", \"COMPLETED_WITH_WARNINGS\", \"INSUFFICIENT_DATA\",\n    \"AGENT_DISAGREEMENT_REQUIRES_REVIEW\",\n})\n\n\ndef _traced_claims():\n    return [\n        {\"kind\": \"supporting\", \"text\": \"Close was 181.2.\",\n         \"source_id\": \"market_report\", \"observation_date\": TRADE_DATE},\n        {\"kind\": \"opposing\", \"text\": \"Trader cautious.\",\n         \"source_id\": \"trader_investment_plan\"},\n    ]\n\n\ndef _untraceable_claims():\n    claims = _traced_claims()\n    claims[0][\"source_id\"] = \"podcast_transcript\"\n    return claims\n\n\ndef _state(**overrides):\n    state = {\n        \"company_of_interest\": \"NVDA\",\n        \"trade_date\": TRADE_DATE,\n        \"market_report\": \"Market report.\",\n        \"sentiment_report\": \"\",\n        \"news_report\": \"\",\n        \"fundamentals_report\": \"\",\n \"llm_provider\": \"openai\",\n        \"deep_think_llm\": \"d\", \"quick_think_llm\": \"q\",\n        \"max_debate_rounds\": 1, \"max_risk_discuss_rounds\": 1,\n        \"output_language\": \"English\", \"data_vendors\": {}, \"tool_vendors\": {},\n        \"claim_enforcement\": enforced,\n    }\n    graph.selected_analysts = (\"market\",)\n    graph.memory_log = TradingMemoryLog({\"memory_log_path\": str(tmp_path / \"m.md\")})\n    return graph\n\n\ndef _logged(tmp_path):\n    path = next(tmp_path.rglob(f\"full_states_log_{TRADE_DATE}.json\"))\n    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n\n# --- The verdict -----------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\ndef test_an_accepted_audit_yields_an_accepted_run():\n    \"\"\"REQ-TA-QR-002: every material claim resolves, so the report is accepted.\"\"\"\n    verdict = evaluate_claim_acceptance(_state(), enforced=True)\n\n    assert verdict[\"status\"] == \"ACCEPTED\"\n    assert verdict[\"audit_status\"] == \"ACCEPTED\"\n    assert verdict[\"enforced\"] is True\n    assert verdict[\"reasons\"] == []\n    assert verdict[\"notice\"] is None\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\ndef test_a_downgraded_audit_rejects_the_run_and_names_reasons(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"REQ-TA-QR-002 / REQ-TA-INV-005: an untraceable test_the_verdict_never_changes_the_final_rating(tmp_path, claims, enforced, expected):\n    state = _state(material_claims=claims, final_rating=\"Underweight\",\n                   final_trade_decision=\"**Rating**: Underweight\")\n    graph = _graph(tmp_path, enforced=enforced)\n\n    graph.record_decision(\"NVDA\", TRADE_DATE, state)\n\n    assert state[\"claim_acceptance\"][\"status\"] == expected\n    assert state[\"final_rating\"] == \"Underweight\"\n    assert _logged(tmp_path)[\"final_rating\"] == \"Underweight\"\n    assert [entry[\"rating\"] for entry in graph.memory_log.load_entries()] == [\"Underweight\"]\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"claims\", [_traced_claims(), _untraceable_claims()])\ndef test_a_review_rating_stays_review_whatever_the_verdict(tmp_path, claims):\n    \"\"\"REQ-TA-INV-001 with REQ-TA-FR-004: the validation gate outranks acceptance.\"\"\"\n    state = _state(material_claims=claims, final_rating=\"REVIEW\",\n                   final_trade_decision=\"No labelled rating here.\")\n    graph = _graph(tmp_path)\n\n    graph.record_decision(\"NVDA\", TRADE_DATE, state)\n\n    assert state[\"final_rating\"] == \"REVIEW\"\n    assert _logged(tmp_path)[\"final_rating\"] == \"REVIEW\"\n\n\n# --- Recording -------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\ndef test_record_decision_stores_the_verdict_in_the_state_log(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"REQ-TA-QR-002: the acceptance verdict is part of the run's record.\"\"\"\n    state = _state(material_claims=_untraceable_claims())\n\n    _graph(tmp_path).record_decision(\"NVDA\", TRADE_DATE, state)\n\n    logged = _logged(tmp_path)[\"claim_acceptance\"]\n    assert logged[\"status\"] == \"REJECTED\"\n    assert logged[\"audit_status\"] == \"DOWNGRADED\"\n    assert logged[\"enforced\"] is True\n    assert logged[\"notice\"] == CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE\n\n\n# --- Both renderings -------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\ndef test_a_rejected_report_states_the_acceptance_and_the_notice(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"REQ-TA-FR-005: the report does not present an untraceable call as accepted.\"\"\"\n    report = write_report_tree(_state(material_claims=_untraceable_claims()), \"NVDA\",\n                               tmp_path).read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    assert \"## Acceptance\" in report\n    assert \"- Acceptance: REJECTED\" in report\n    assert CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE in report\n    assert report.index(\"## Acceptance\") < report.index(\"II. Research Team Decision\")\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\ndef test_a_rejected_html_report_states_the_acceptance(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"REQ-TA-FR-005: the HTML page says what the Markdown report says.\"\"\"\n    write_report_tree(_state(material_claims=_untraceable_claims()), \"NVDA\", tmp_path)\n    page = (tmp_path / \"complete_report.html\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    assert \">REJECTED<\" in page\n    assert CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE in page\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\ndef test_an_accepted_report_carries_no_non_acceptance_notice(tmp_path):\n    write_report_tree(_state(), \"NVDA\", tmp_path)\n    report = (tmp_path / \"complete_report.md\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    page = (tmp_path / \"complete_report.html\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    assert \"## Acceptance\" not in report\n    assert \"Acceptance:\" not in report\n    assert CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE not in report\n    assert CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE not in page\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\ndef test_a_report_without_a_decision_gets_no_acceptance_block(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"Nothing to accept: a failed run is reported as such, not as a rejection.\"\"\"\n    state = _state(final_trade_decision=None, final_rating=\"REVIEW\", material_claims=None)\n\n    report = write_report_tree(state, \"NVDA\", tmp_path).read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    assert \"## Acceptance\" not in report\n\n\n# --- Boundaries ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@pytest.mark.unit\ndef test_the_verdict_never_uses_a_run_status_or_an_audit_status():\n    \"\"\"The three vocabularies are disjoint: acceptance is its own thing.\"\"\"\n    assert CLAIM_ACCEPTANCE_STATUSES == {\"ACCEPTED\", \"REJECTED\", \"NOT_ENFORCED\"}\n    assert CLAIM_ACCEPTANCE_STATUSES & _FR_006_STATUSES == set()\n    assert \"ACCEPTED\" in {audit_material_claims(_state())[\"status\"]}\n    assert audit_material_claims(_state())[\"status\"] != \"NOT_ENFORCED\"\n"
```

"""Shared pytest fixtures that prevent CI hangs when API keys are absent."""

import os
import socket

import pytest


def _blank_settings_overlay():
    """Blank every TRADINGAGENTS_* setting before the package is imported.

    The package loads .env on import and folds these variables into
    DEFAULT_CONFIG, so a contributor's own settings would become the defaults
    the suite asserts on. A blank value is still present, so load_dotenv leaves
    it alone, and the overlay reads it as unset. Tests of the overlay set their own.
    """
    from dotenv import dotenv_values, find_dotenv

    names = set(os.environ)
    for filename in (".env", ".env.enterprise"):
        names |= set(dotenv_values(find_dotenv(filename, usecwd=True)))
    for name in names:
        if name.startswith("TRADINGAGENTS_"):
            os.environ[name] = ""


_blank_settings_overlay()


def _own_file_locations():
    """Keep the suite's results, cache and memory log in a directory of its own.

    Set before the package is imported, whose defaults put them in the user's
    home: a test reading the user's cache would see what their runs fetched.
    """
    import tempfile

    home = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="tradingagents-tests-")
    os.environ["TRADINGAGENTS_RESULTS_DIR"] = os.path.join(home, "logs")
    os.environ["TRADINGAGENTS_CACHE_DIR"] = os.path.join(home, "cache")
    os.environ["TRADINGAGENTS_MEMORY_LOG_PATH"] = os.path.join(home, "memory", "trading_memory.md")


_own_file_locations()


def pytest_configure(config):
    for marker in ("unit", "integration", "smoke"):
        config.addinivalue_line("markers", f"{marker}: {marker}-level tests")
    # yfinance caches each symbol's time zone on disk, in the home directory
    # by default; the suite keeps it in a directory of its own.
    import tempfile

    import yfinance

    yfinance.set_tz_cache_location(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="tradingagents-tests-yf-"))


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _no_network(request, monkeypatch):
    """Tests do not reach the network; one that must is marked integration."""
    if request.node.get_closest_marker("integration"):
        return

    def refuse(*args, **kwargs):
        raise OSError(f"test tried to reach the network: {args[1:] or kwargs}")

    monkeypatch.setattr(socket.socket, "connect", refuse)
    monkeypatch.setattr(socket.socket, "connect_ex", refuse)
    monkeypatch.setattr(socket, "getaddrinfo", lambda *a, **k: refuse(None, *a))
    # yfinance's HTTP client is libcurl, which never touches Python's sockets.
    from curl_cffi import requests as curl_requests

    monkeypatch.setattr(curl_requests.Session, "request", refuse)
    monkeypatch.setattr(curl_requests.AsyncSession, "request", refuse)


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _at_a_terminal(monkeypatch):
    """Tests of the interactive steps run as if at a terminal; pytest's stdin is
    not one. A test of an unattended run sets isatty to False itself."""
    import sys

    monkeypatch.setattr(sys.stdin, "isatty", lambda: True)


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _own_cli_prefs(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """The CLI keeps the last run's selections in the user's home; tests keep theirs apart."""
    monkeypatch.setattr("cli.prefs._PREFS_PATH", tmp_path / "cli_prefs.json")


_API_KEY_ENV_VARS = (
    "OPENAI_API_KEY",
    "GOOGLE_API_KEY",
    "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY",
    "XAI_API_KEY",
    "DEEPSEEK_API_KEY",
    "DASHSCOPE_API_KEY",

    "AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY",
    "ALPHA_VANTAGE_API_KEY",
)


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _dummy_api_keys(monkeypatch):
    for env_var in _API_KEY_ENV_VARS:
        # `or` not a .get default: an env var present but empty (e.g. a key left
        # blank in a .env copied from .env.example) must still get the placeholder.
        monkeypatch.setenv(env_var, os.environ.get(env_var) or "placeholder")


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _isolate_config():
    """Reset the global dataflows config before and after each test.

    ``set_config`` merges (it never clears keys absent from the override), so a
    test that sets e.g. ``tool_vendors`` would otherwise leak into later tests
    and make routing behavior order-dependent. Replace the global outright so
    every test starts from a clean DEFAULT_CONFIG.
    """
    import copy

    import tradingagents.dataflows.config as config_module
    import tradingagents.default_config as default_config

    config_module._config = copy.deepcopy(default_config.DEFAULT_CONFIG)
    yield
    config_module._config = copy.deepcopy(default_config.DEFAULT_CONFIG)

@@ -216,6 +237,53 @@ def audit_material_claims(final_state: dict) -> dict:
     }
 
 
+def evaluate_claim_acceptance(final_state: dict, *, enforced: bool) -> dict:
+    """Derive the run's acceptance verdict from its claim audit (ADR-001).
+
+    Pure: no I/O and no LLM. Implements REQ-TA-QR-002 and REQ-TA-INV-005: an
+    untraceable material claim — or no claim metadata at all — prevents
+    acceptance. With enforcement off the verdict says so instead of claiming
+    acceptance. It never reads or writes the rating (REQ-TA-INV-001).
+    """
+    audit = audit_material_claims(final_state)
+    reasons = list(audit["reasons"])
+    if not enforced:
+        status, notice = "NOT_ENFORCED", None
+        if "CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT_DISABLED" not in reasons:
+            reasons.append("CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT_DISABLED")
+    elif audit["status"] == "ACCEPTED":
+        status, notice = "ACCEPTED", None
+    else:
+        status, notice = "REJECTED", CLAIM_NOT_ACCEPTED_NOTICE
+    return {
+        "status": status,
+        "audit_status": audit["status"],
+        "enforced": enforced,
+        "reasons": reasons,
+        "notice": notice,
+    }
+
+
+def claim_acceptance_of(final_state: dict) -> dict:
+    """The verdict recorded for this run, or the one this state would get.
+
+    A state that never went through ``record_decision`` (a hand-built state, a
+    partial run) is judged with enforcement on, so a report cannot render as
+    accepted by omission.
+    """
+    recorded = final_state.get("claim_acceptance")
+    if isinstance(recorded, dict) and recorded.get("status") in CLAIM_ACCEPTANCE_STATUSES:
+        return recorded
+    return evaluate_claim_acceptance(final_state, enforced=True)
+
+
+def acceptance_notice_text(verdict: dict) -> str | None:
+    """The sentence a rendering shows for a verdict that is not ``ACCEPTED``."""
+    if verdict.get("status") == "ACCEPTED":
+        return None
+    return verdict.get("notice") or CLAIM_NOT_CERTIFIED_NOTICE
+
+
 def render_claim_ledger(final_state: dict) -> str:
     """The markdown claim ledger: each factor with its source, date or UNSUPPORTED label."""
     items = _claim_items(final_state)

**Verdict vocabulary as approved** — `ACCEPTED` / `REJECTED` / `NOT_ENFORCED`, with `audit_status`, `enforced`, `reasons`, `notice` alongside. `NOT_ASSESSED` maps to `REJECTED` under enforcement; with enforcement off the verdict says `NOT_ENFORCED` and can never read as acceptance. A boundary test asserts the verdict shares no value with the REQ-TA-FR-006 enum, which remains out of scope.

**Rendered output (rejected run, verified by eye and by test):**

```
- Traceability: DOWNGRADED (0/2 material claims attributed); UNKNOWN_SOURCE
- Acceptance: REJECTED — UNKNOWN_SOURCE

## Acceptance

**REJECTED** — This report is not accepted as an evidence-linked recommendation: …
```

The block precedes section I, so the verdict is met before the recommendation. A run with **no** claim metadata has no ledger, so this block is the only place its non-acceptance appears — that case is tested explicitly. A report with no decision at all gets no acceptance block (nothing to accept).

1: """Portfolio Manager: synthesises the risk-analyst debate into the final decision.
2: 
3: Uses LangChain's ``with_structured_output`` so the LLM produces a typed
4: ``PortfolioDecision`` directly, in a single call. Its rating is the run's
5: ``final_rating``, and the decision is rendered to markdown as
6: ``final_trade_decision`` for the memory log, CLI display and saved reports.
7: The node also returns ``material_claims``: the structured supporting and
8: opposing factors, each linked to a report artifact (ADR-001).
9: When a provider does not expose structured output, the agent falls back to
10: free-text generation and the rating is read from that text.
11: """
12: 
13: from __future__ import annotations
14: 
15: import logging
16: 
17: from tradingagents.agents.context import (
18:     get_instrument_context_from_state,
19:     get_language_instruction,
20:     get_portfolio_context_from_state,
21: )
22: from tradingagents.agents.rating import parse_rating
23: from tradingagents.agents.schemas import (
24:     CLAIM_SOURCE_IDS,
25:     PortfolioDecision,
26:     render_pm_decision,
27: )
28: from tradingagents.agents.structured import NO_EXTERNAL_TOOLS, bind_structured, invoke_structured
29: 
30: logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
31: 
32: 
33: def _invoke_json_mode(llm, prompt) -> PortfolioDecision | None:
34:     """Retry the decision as JSON before free text (deepseek-flash misses tool calls).
35: 
36:     A json_mode result is still a structured decision, so the claims/rating path
37:     is unchanged and ``claim_fallback`` stays ``None`` (ADR-001).
38:     """
39:     try:
40:         structured_json = llm.with_structured_output(PortfolioDecision, method="json_mode")
41:     except (NotImplementedError, AttributeError) as exc:
42:         logger.warning("PM: json_mode retry failed (binding: %s)", exc)
43:         return None
44:     try:
45:         result = structured_json.invoke(prompt)
46:         if result is None:
47:             raise ValueError("json_mode returned no result")
48:         if isinstance(result, PortfolioDecision):
49:             return result
50:         return PortfolioDecision.model_validate_json(result)
51:     except Exception as exc:
52:         logger.warning("PM: json_mode retry failed (%s)", exc)
53:         return None
54: 
55: 
56: def create_portfolio_manager(llm):
57:     structured_llm = bind_structured(llm, PortfolioDecision, "Portfolio Manager")
58: 
59:     def portfolio_manager_node(state) -> dict:
60:         instrument_context = get_instrument_context_from_state(state)
61:         portfolio_context = get_portfolio_context_from_state(state)
62: 
63:         history = state["risk_debate_state"]["history"]
64:         risk_debate_state = state["risk_debate_state"]
65:         research_plan = state["investment_plan"]
66:         trader_plan = state["trader_investment_plan"]
67: 
68:         past_context = state.get("past_context", "")
69:         lessons_line = (
70:             f"- Lessons from prior decisions and outcomes:\n{past_context}\n"
71:             if past_context
72:             else ""
73:         )
74: 
75:         prompt = f"""As the Portfolio Manager, synthesize the risk analysts' debate and deliver the final trading decision.
76: 
77: {instrument_context}
78: 
79: {portfolio_context}
80: 
81: ---
82: 
83: **Rating Scale** (use exactly one):
84: - **Buy**: Strong conviction to enter or add to position
85: - **Overweight**: Favorable outlook, gradually increase exposure
86: - **Hold**: Maintain current position, no action needed
87: - **Underweight**: Reduce exposure, take partial profits
88: - **Sell**: Exit position or avoid entry
89: 
90: **Context:**
91: - Research Manager's investment plan: **{research_plan}**
92: - Trader's transaction proposal: **{trader_plan}**
93: {lessons_line}
94: **Risk Analysts Debate History:**
95: {history}
96: 
97: ---
98: 
99: Ground every conclusion in specific evidence from the analysts. The risk debate always contains conflicting stances; deciding which is stronger is the job, so conflict alone is not a reason to Hold. Commit to the stronger case, sized by how decisively it wins. Choose Hold only when the evidence is still balanced after that weighing, or too thin to support a call; do not force a direction to appear decisive. Weigh the analysts on their merits, independent of speaking order.
100: 
101: ## Output
102: 
103: Write these sections, in this order, starting with the rating on its own line:
104: 
105: - **Rating**: exactly one of Buy / Overweight / Hold / Underweight / Sell
106: - **Confidence**: exactly one of low / medium / high
107: - **Executive Summary**: the call and how to act on it
108: - **Investment Thesis**: the evidence that decided it, and what would change it
109: - **Supporting Factors**: exactly three material claims that support the rating
110: - **Opposing Factors**: exactly three material claims that argue against it
111: 
112: Set **Confidence** from the evidence and the analysts' disagreement:
113: - **low**: the evidence is thin, or the analysts strongly disagree.
114: - **medium**: the evidence is adequate but mixed, or disagreement is material.
115: - **high**: the evidence is strong and consistent across analysts, with only
116:   minor disagreement.
117: 
118: For every factor, give one `source_id` naming the report it came from, chosen
119: from: {CLAIM_SOURCE_IDS}. Give `observation_date` as YYYY-MM-DD whenever the
120: claim asserts market data (a price or indicator); omit it for qualitative
121: claims. Do not name the producing agent: it is derived from the source.
122: 
123: {NO_EXTERNAL_TOOLS}{get_language_instruction()}"""
124: 
125:         # The typed rating is the decision; the rendered text only carries it.
126:         # Read back from text, a rating the thesis quotes could replace it.
127:         decision = invoke_structured(structured_llm, prompt, "Portfolio Manager")
128:         if decision is None:
129:             decision = _invoke_json_mode(llm, prompt)
130:         claims = []
131:         if decision is not None:
132:             final_trade_decision = render_pm_decision(decision)
133:             final_rating = decision.rating.value
134:             # ADR-001: a material claim is exactly an item in these two lists.
135:             claims = [
136:                 {
137:                     "kind": kind,
138:                     "text": claim.text,
139:                     "source_id": claim.source_id,
140:                     "observation_date": claim.observation_date,
141:                 }
142:                 for kind, factors in (
143:                     ("supporting", decision.supporting_factors),
144:                     ("opposing", decision.opposing_factors),
145:                 )
146:                 for claim in factors
147:             ]
148:         else:
149:             final_trade_decision = llm.invoke(prompt).content
150:             final_rating = parse_rating(final_trade_decision)
151: 
152:         new_risk_debate_state = {
153:             "history": risk_debate_state["history"],
154:             "aggressive_history": risk_debate_state["aggressive_history"],
155:             "conservative_history": risk_debate_state["conservative_history"],
156:             "neutral_history": risk_debate_state["neutral_history"],
157:             "latest_speaker": "Judge",
158:             "current_aggressive_response": risk_debate_state["current_aggressive_response"],
159:             "current_conservative_response": risk_debate_state["current_conservative_response"],
160:             "current_neutral_response": risk_debate_state["current_neutral_response"],
161:             "count": risk_debate_state["count"],
162:         }
163: 
164:         return {
165:             "risk_debate_state": new_risk_debate_state,
166:             "final_trade_decision": final_trade_decision,
167:             "final_rating": final_rating,
168:             "material_claims": claims,
169:             # ADR-001: makes a structured-output miss visible to the audit,
170:             # which otherwise cannot tell it from a model that emitted no claims.
171:             "claim_fallback": None if decision is not None else "free_text",
172:         }
173: 
174:     return portfolio_manager_node

190: def report_or_absent(text: str, source: str) -> str:
191:     """An analyst's report, or a marker saying it was never produced.

193:     A report is empty when its analyst was not selected, refused, or returned
194:     nothing. Interpolating that into a labelled section presents an absence as a

196:     an empty opponent argument used to invite an invented rebuttal (#1176).
197:     """
198:     text = (text or "").strip()
199:     if text:
200:         return text
201:     return f"(No {source} report in this run: it is not available, not an empty finding.)"

36: def _prompt_text(prompt) -> str:
37:     """Flatten a captured prompt (str, message list, or objects) to text."""

39:         return prompt

42:         parts.append(m.get("content", "") if isinstance(m, dict) else getattr(m, "content", ""))
43:     return "\n".join(str(p) for p in parts)

19:         instrument_context: str = "",
20:         portfolio_context: str = "",
21:     ) -> dict[str, Any]:
22:         """Create the initial state for the agent graph.

24:         ``instrument_context`` is the deterministic ticker-identity string
25:         resolved once at run start (see

28:         ``get_instrument_context_from_state``.
29:         """

31:             "messages": [("human", company_name)],
32:             "company_of_interest": company_name,
33:             "asset_type": asset_type,
34:             "instrument_context": instrument_context,
35:             "trade_date": str(trade_date),

Good. Note the f-string now has `{", ".join(...)}` inside — an f-string expression containing a double-quoted string literal... In Python 3.12+ that's allowed (PEP 701); target is py311 per ruff config. In Python 3.11, f-string expressions cannot reuse the same quote type as the enclosing string. The enclosing triple-quoted string is `f"""..."""`, so a `"` inside the expression is fine in 3.11 because the delimiter is `"""` and the inner quote is single `"`. Actually yes: `f"""... {", ".join(x)} ..."""` is valid in 3.11 — the restriction is on reusing the *same* quote character sequence; a single `"` inside `"""` is fine.

But ruff target-version py311 — fine.

```python
def _pm_state(**overrides):
    state = {
        "company_of_interest": "NVDA",
        "risk_debate_state": {
            "history": "RISK-DEBATE-MARK", "aggressive_history": "a",
            "conservative_history": "c", "neutral_history": "n",
            "current_aggressive_response": "", "current_conservative_response": "",
            "current_neutral_response": "", "latest_speaker": "Neutral", "count": 1,
        },
        "investment_plan": "plan",
        "trader_investment_plan": "trader plan",
        "market_report": "MARKET-MARK: close 189.5",
        "sentiment_report": "SENTIMENT-MARK: bullish",
        "news_report": "NEWS-MARK: guidance raised",
        "fundamentals_report": "FUNDAMENTALS-MARK: margin 74%",
    }
    state.update(overrides)
    return state


def _pm_decision():
    from tradingagents.agents.schemas import PortfolioDecision, PortfolioRating
    return PortfolioDecision(rating=PortfolioRating.HOLD, confidence="medium",
                             executive_summary="x", investment_thesis="y")


def _pm_prompt(state):
    captured = {}
    create_portfolio_manager(_capturing_llm(captured, _pm_decision()))(state)
    return _prompt_text(captured["prompt"])
```

```python
@pytest.mark.unit
def test_portfolio_manager_prompt_carries_primary_analyst_evidence():
    text = _pm_prompt(_pm_state())

    assert "Primary analyst evidence" in text
    for marker in ("MARKET-MARK", "SENTIMENT-MARK", "NEWS-MARK", "FUNDAMENTALS-MARK"):
        assert marker in text
    for source in PRIMARY_ANALYST_SOURCES:
        assert f"`{source}`" in text
```

feat(FR-005): require a primary analyst citation

The Portfolio Manager saw only the risk debate's paraphrase of the analyst
work, so every material claim could legitimately cite the debate, the
research plan or the trader — and NO_ANALYST_REFERENCE would downgrade the
run with no way for the model to have known why.

The prompt now carries the four analyst reports under a "Primary analyst
evidence" section before the risk debate history, each labelled with the
source_id a claim cites, and a report the run never produced is marked
absent (report_or_absent) rather than blank. One of the six material claims
must cite market_report, sentiment_report, news_report or
fundamentals_report; the prompt says so and says the run is downgraded
otherwise.

The audit and fail-closed enforcement are unchanged: NO_ANALYST_REFERENCE
still decides, this only tells the model what it is being asked for.

## Change

**`src/tradingagents/agents/managers/portfolio_manager.py`**

- `PRIMARY_ANALYST_REPORTS` / `PRIMARY_ANALYST_SOURCES`: one table of the four analyst reports (name, absent-marker word, `source_id`), so the prompt, the tests and the claim vocabulary cannot drift apart.
- `primary_analyst_evidence(state)`: renders each report as ``- **Market Analyst** (`market_report`): …`` through `report_or_absent`, so a report the run never produced reads *"(No news report in this run: it is not available, not an empty finding.)"* rather than as a blank finding. Reads via `state.get(...)` so a partial state (bare programmatic states, prompt tests) still builds.
- New prompt section **Primary analyst evidence:** inserted between the Context block and **Risk Analysts Debate History:**.

  ```text
forbids any fetch. Report text is rendered as CommonMark with raw HTML and images
off, so nothing a model wrote can inject markup or load a remote asset.
"""
  ```

  ```text
  ### Checkpoint signature

`_run_signature` uses the resolved analysts and round caps; it appends a `tools=`
segment only when the effective tool cap differs from the caller's config value.
A legacy/omitted-profile call therefore keeps the pre-change signature for any
tool cap (20 or a custom one), while a profile or explicit override that changes
the analysts or rounds yields a distinct signature and never resumes a stale
checkpoint.
  ```
